*Objective*: Discriminate a SYS_OUT write hang: AW vs W at both ends.

*Test Coverage*:

* Discriminate a SYS_OUT write hang: AW vs W at both ends.
* After the outbound filter is programmed pass-all, one JTAG AXI write is issued at the SYS_OUT responder window (0x0200_0000).
* A clocked sticky observer records whether AW/W/B ever handshook on the JTAG ingress and on the SYS_OUT boundary.
* Healthy path: write completes and every channel handshook.
* The hang under test is AW-at-SYS_OUT without W-at-SYS_OUT after JTAG W was accepted.
* Those two outcomes are different assertion texts so a bare timeout cannot be mistaken for the split.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_sys_out_aw_w_split_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SYS-OUT-WR-COMPLETE` |Named compare in `smc_sys_out_aw_w_split_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-SYS-OUT-WR-COMPLETE` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-037-smc_xvisor_remap_test]]
===== 37 `smc_xvisor_remap_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_xvisor_remap_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Hypervisor remap table (0xC001_4000) sweep.

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC_XVISOR_REMAP full sweep over the 8-entry hypervisor remap table the generated map declares (SMC_XVISOR_REMAP_0..7, ATTRS-only per entry), a direct sibling of the ALIAS_REMAP and MMODE_REMAP tables that `smc_remap_cla_test` sweeps.
* Reset content: every entry reads its generated RDL reset as a scoreboard value compare with an OKAY response.
* Per-entry decode: all eight entries hold distinct patterns inside the `offset` field at the same time and each reads back its own, so an entry aliased onto a neighbour or an undecoded window answering OKAY with zeros fails the readback; every entry is restored to its reset and re-read. A reset read alone cannot make that distinction, since the reset word is zero.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_xvisor_remap_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-XVISOR-REMAP-CORESIDENT` |All eight entries hold distinct `offset` patterns written before any is read back, each reads back its own pattern and then its RDL reset after the restore write, every readback a scoreboard value compare; an aliased pair or an OKAY-zero hole fails the first readback of the pair
|`CHK-XVISOR-REMAP-RESET-DEFAULT` |All eight `SMC_XVISOR_REMAP_<n>` REGION_ATTRS 64-bit reads over SEP_IN AXI return OKAY and the full RDL reset word as scoreboard value compares; a non-OKAY response, a non-reset value or a short sweep fails
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-XVISOR-REMAP-CORESIDENT` and `CHK-XVISOR-REMAP-RESET-DEFAULT` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds, including the exact access count of 40. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-179-smc_address_map_region_decode_test]]
===== 179 `smc_address_map_region_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_address_map_region_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP); `tb_axil_external_active`, a mirror of `smc_external_req_o`, sampled on every `clk_smc_i` edge for the adopter external AXI-Lite window

*Objective*: Every address-map region an inbound SEP_IN master can reach decodes at a base-side register and at the last register of its extent; three addresses no block declares are answered DECERR by the fabric error slave; the external window is proven at its own AXI-Lite port.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every expectation comes from the generated register map (`hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/py/smc_reg.py`) or from a co-resident write/readback pattern, never from a first read of the DUT. A region whose reset is zero throughout is proven by the co-resident pattern rather than by a 0 == 0 compare, because a dead or unmapped window commonly reads 0.
* The co-resident shape writes several addresses before any of them is read back, so a decode that collapses two windows onto one physical register returns a neighbour's pattern and fails the exact compare -- which a per-address write-then-read cannot see.
* Three addresses no generated-map block declares and no crossbar rule covers (past the watchdog cluster, past the DFX block, past the fabric-control block) are declared expected-error to the SEP_IN monitor and required to answer DECERR from the fabric error slave.
* The APB4-fronted blocks are read at their 32-bit generated-map addresses, and 32-bit reads are required to return values with bits above 16 set, so the APB4 data path is exercised beyond a 16-bit window.
* The regions SEP_IN cannot reach in this bench are named in the log and left open rather than claimed: the PLIC and CLINT/BEU windows fold onto the local base in `smc_local_fabric`, the generated map declares no register in the debug-module region, and the M-mode/Xvisor remap regions are outbound apertures with no inbound decode pinned by the specification.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_address_map_region_decode_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer. The sequence sweeps every reachable region at its base side and at the top of its extent, probes the three beyond-region addresses, routes one access into the external window, requires the scoreboard's exact-value compare count to have advanced by its floor, and prints the cell ledger. The leaf then re-checks the scoreboard compare count and books a protocol-VIP record with the sequence's measured access count against a stimulus floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ADDRESS-MAP-REGION` |The cell ledger. One line per coverage cell the sequence closed, naming the measured evidence that closed it, plus one line per cell left NOT closed in this bench with the reason. A cell is entered only after its own compare passed, and the ledger raises if no cell closed at all; the NOT-closed lines are recorded gaps, not compares
|`CHK-ADDRESS-MAP-REGION-DECODE` |Summary line: cells closed, the scoreboard exact-value compare count against its floor, the number of beyond-region DECERR probes, the external-port active cycles and the number of cells left open. Logged only once every per-region compare and the compare-count floor have held; it carries no compare of its own
|`CHK-ADDRESS-MAP-REGION-FLOOR` |The leaf requires the scoreboard's `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged, so a run whose stimulus stopped reaching the DUT fails instead of passing with a short sweep
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every region's generated-reset compare or co-resident readback holds, the three beyond-region addresses each answer DECERR, the external window access drives `smc_external_req_o`, and the scoreboard books at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` exact-value compares. The cells named NOT closed are not part of the claim. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-180-smc_address_window_top_decode_test]]
===== 180 `smc_address_window_top_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_address_window_top_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: The last word of every SEP_IN-reachable window `memmap.adoc` pins -- through the table the register generator writes from the RDL address map -- answers, and answers from its own block: a window sized short bus-errors there and one sized long is answered by the next block.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every window top is derived from the generated memory-map table (`regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, included into `memmap.adoc`) and `LOCAL_BASE` from the generated `SMC_BASE_CONFIG.LOCAL_BASE` reset; no window extent is hand-copied.
* Scratchpad memory: the first and the last 64-bit word of the 1 MiB `spm_memory` window hold distinct co-resident patterns and each reads back exactly, so the window reaches its last word without aliasing.
* Boot ROM: the last word of the 128 KiB `spm_rom_memory` window (the extent `rom.adoc` states) answers OKAY, returns neither resident scratchpad pattern, refuses a write with an error response, and still holds the same word afterwards -- which is what read-only means at this boundary. No specification pins the word an unprogrammed ROM offset holds, so that value is reported, not compared.
* DMA controller and memory zeroer: with distinct patterns resident in `DMA_CTRL.DST_ADDRESS_LO` and `ZEROER_CTRL.DEST_ADDR`, the last word of the 512 B `dma_ctrl` window and of the 256 B `zeroer_ctrl` window answers OKAY and returns neither pattern. Both words lie past the registers each unit implements and no specification pins them, so their values are reported, not compared.
* DMA stream banks: `NEXT_ID_1` through `NEXT_ID_15` each answer OKAY with exactly 0 as `dma.adoc` states for a reserved bank, each checked to sit at the 8-byte stream-bank stride from `NEXT_ID_0`. `NEXT_ID_0` is read last and for an OKAY response only, because reading it is the one access that launches a transfer. The reserved banks hold no writable state (`STATUS` and `DONE` are tied to 0 too), so this leg proves that every reserved bank address answers as specified and does not claim to tell one reserved bank from another.
* CLA: the trace-sink `TRDSTRAMIMPL` identification word, in the first 8 bytes of the 16 KiB `smc_cla` window, reads its generated reset, and the window's last 8-byte word -- the trace destination's `ScratchLo`/`ScratchHi` pair -- holds distinct co-resident patterns and reads them back.
* One window top is not a register: the miscellaneous wrapper's 2 KiB window carries 524 B of registers, so its last word is an in-window offset past the register file. It is read with the error response tolerated and left open with its measured response -- a recorded gap, not a claim.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_address_window_top_decode_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer, then let the leaf re-check the scoreboard's exact-value compare count and book a protocol-VIP record against a stimulus floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ADDRESS-WINDOW-TOP` |The cell ledger: one line per window top closed with the measured evidence, plus one line for the top left NOT closed with the response it actually gave. Cells are entered only after their compare passed; the NOT-closed line is a recorded gap
|`CHK-ADDRESS-WINDOW-TOP-DECODE` |Summary line: window tops closed, the scoreboard exact-value compare count against its floor, the access count, and how many tops are in-window offsets past their unit's registers. Logged after the per-window compares and the floor have held
|`CHK-ADDRESS-WINDOW-TOP-FLOOR` |The leaf requires `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every closed window top answered OKAY; the scratchpad, CLA and stream-bank tops matched their co-resident pattern, generated reset or specified 0 exactly; the ROM, DMA and zeroer tops returned neither resident pattern and have no specified value to compare; the ROM write was refused and left the word unchanged; and the scoreboard booked at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` compares. The miscellaneous-wrapper top is reported, not claimed. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-181-smc_alias_remap_spare_block_decode_test]]
===== 181 `smc_alias_remap_spare_block_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_alias_remap_spare_block_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: `LOCAL_BASE` reaches the SMC's own resources through the alias remap's reset setting, and the ends of the eight-entry alias-remap array, the two spare register blocks and the four end mailbox halves each decode as separate storage.

*Test Coverage*:

* The first access of the run is a read at `LOCAL_BASE` itself -- the core-0 watchdog `CTRL` sits at window offset 0 -- taken before anything is written to the remap block, so the same access carries both the local-alias claim and the transparent-at-reset claim. `CMP` in the same block carries a non-zero generated reset, which is what makes the offset-0 read decode evidence rather than a 0 == 0 compare.
* Alias-remap regions 0 and 7, the ends of the array, are checked to sit at the chapter's 0x20 stride, configured with distinct co-resident `REGION_START` values and restored to the generated reset, so a decoder that collapses the eight instances onto one fails the readback.
* Spare blocks: the scratch word holds an all-ones pattern and reads it back exactly, so all 32 bits are storage; the chip-config block is written and must still read its generated reset, because every `chip_config.rdl` field is `sw = r`.
* Mailbox: the two halves of pair 0 and of pair 31 hold four distinct co-resident `IRQEN` patterns at once, then read back 0. The offset of each half inside a pair comes from the generated map.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_alias_remap_spare_block_decode_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer, then let the leaf re-check the scoreboard's exact-value compare count and book a protocol-VIP record against a stimulus floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-SPARE-BLOCK` |The cell ledger: one line per cell closed with the measured evidence behind it. A cell is entered only after its compare passed, and the ledger raises if no cell closed
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-SPARE-BLOCK-DECODE` |Summary line: cells closed, accesses issued and the scoreboard exact-value compare count against its floor, naming the `LOCAL_BASE` read taken before any remap write, the two alias-array ends, the two spare blocks and the four mailbox halves. Logged after those compares and the floor have held
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-SPARE-BLOCK-FLOOR` |The leaf requires `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the `LOCAL_BASE` and `CMP` reads match the generated resets, every co-resident pattern reads back exactly and every register is restored, the chip-config write leaves the register at its reset, and the scoreboard books at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-185-smc_dual_base_addressing_test]]
===== 185 `smc_dual_base_addressing_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dual_base_addressing_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: `LOCAL_BASE` is fixed read-only at 0xC000_0000 and keeps that value across a write attempt, `GLOBAL_BASE` in the same block takes and returns a pattern through the identical write path, and `REGION_SIZE` reads its specified 16 MiB reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* The three expectations are the sentences of `memmap.adoc` (Memory Map); nothing is read back from the DUT to form them.
* The write-has-no-effect leg has a positive control in the same register block: `GLOBAL_BASE`, the programmable sibling, is written and read back through the same path, so a dropped `LOCAL_BASE` write is attributable to the read-only attribute and not to a dead write channel. The control is checked not to already hold the probe pattern, and `GLOBAL_BASE` is restored to the value read at entry.
* `LOCAL_BASE` is written last, so a DUT that wrongly accepted the write fails its own readback rather than silently re-basing every later access of the run.
* `smc_region_size_o` is unconnected in `tb_top`, so the pin-side effect of `REGION_SIZE` is not claimed here.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_dual_base_addressing_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer: read `REGION_SIZE` and `LOCAL_BASE` against their specified values, save `GLOBAL_BASE` and prove the write path on it, then attempt the `LOCAL_BASE` write and require the register to still read 0xC000_0000. The leaf re-checks the scoreboard compare count and books a protocol-VIP record against a stimulus floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DUAL-BASE` |The cell ledger: `local-base-reads-c0000000` and `local-base-write-has-no-effect`, each with the measured evidence behind it, including the `GLOBAL_BASE` write that serves as the positive control
|`CHK-DUAL-BASE-ADDRESSING` |Summary line: the `LOCAL_BASE` value across the write attempt, the `GLOBAL_BASE` save/pattern/restore sequence and the `REGION_SIZE` reset. Logged after those compares and the stimulus floor have held
|`CHK-DUAL-BASE-FLOOR` |The leaf requires the scoreboard's `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `REGION_SIZE` reads the specified 16 MiB reset, `LOCAL_BASE` reads 0xC000_0000 before and after the write attempt, `GLOBAL_BASE` returns the probe pattern and then the restored value, and the scoreboard books at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-190-smc_region_size_plic_clint_beu_decode_test]]
===== 190 `smc_region_size_plic_clint_beu_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_region_size_plic_clint_beu_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: With `REGION_SIZE` programmed to 256 MiB the PLIC, the CLINT and the four bus error units decode at the addresses the chapters give them; at the 16 MiB reset those addresses lie above the local aperture, where the specification does not define the response, so what they answer there is reported and not claimed.

*Test Coverage*:

* `REGION_SIZE` reads its generated reset and `WDT0.CMP` inside the reset aperture reads its generated reset. The PLIC priority word at the same window offset is read above the reset aperture with the response tolerated: `fabric.adoc` and `memmap.adoc` size the aperture but do not say what an inbound access above it returns, so the answer is recorded in the cell ledger and not compared. The proof that the aperture widened is the PLIC, CLINT and BEU legs themselves: co-resident patterns and generated resets at addresses that are outside the aperture at reset.
* 256 MiB is the smallest legal power of two whose aperture contains the whole documented map up to BASE + 0x801_3FFF; the sequence asserts that relation against the generated reset size rather than hard-coding a verdict.
* PLIC: the priority and per-core enable words hold distinct co-resident patterns, are then written to the priority-0 / disabled state `interrupts.adoc` requires of firmware and read back exactly; the top word of the 4 MiB window and the first word above it must answer without returning either pattern.
* CLINT: `MSIP_0` and `MTIMECMP_0` hold distinct co-resident patterns, and the top word of the 64 KiB window must not return them.
* BEU: the four `ENABLE` registers read their generated reset, are each checked against the specified 0x1000 stride, and then hold four distinct co-resident patterns, so the per-core instances are proven separate registers rather than one alias.
* `REGION_SIZE` is restored to its generated reset and a local-window register is read afterwards, so the aperture is left as the rest of the suite expects.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_region_size_plic_clint_beu_decode_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer: read `REGION_SIZE` and a local-window register at reset and record what the PLIC word answers above the aperture, widen `REGION_SIZE`, drive the PLIC, its window edges, the CLINT and the four BEUs, restore `REGION_SIZE` and re-read a local-window register, then require the exact-value compare floor and print the cell ledger. The leaf re-checks the scoreboard compare count and books a protocol-VIP record.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-REGION-SIZE-PLIC-CLINT-BEU` |The cell ledger: one line per cell closed with the measured evidence -- the reset-aperture reads with the recorded above-aperture answer, the PLIC base, edge and firmware-init cells, the CLINT base and top, and the four BEU instances. A cell is entered only after its compare passed
|`CHK-REGION-SIZE-PLIC-CLINT-BEU-DECODE` |Summary line: the response and word the PLIC address gave above the reset aperture (reported), the word it gave at 256 MiB, cells closed, accesses issued and the scoreboard exact-value compare count against its floor. Logged after those compares and the floor have held
|`CHK-REGION-SIZE-PLIC-CLINT-BEU-FLOOR` |The leaf requires the scoreboard's `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `REGION_SIZE` and `WDT0.CMP` read their generated resets at the reset aperture, every PLIC, CLINT and BEU co-resident pattern reads back exactly at 256 MiB, the window-edge reads return neither resident pattern, `REGION_SIZE` is restored and the local-window register reads its reset afterwards, and the scoreboard books at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-192-smc_sys_axi_in_port_test]]
===== 192 `smc_sys_axi_in_port_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_sys_axi_in_port_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP_IN AXI driver for the filter programming and the readbacks, plus the SYS_IN AXI agent (`sys_in_axi_agent`) driving `sys_axi_in_req_i`

*Objective*: Once inbound filter entry 0 admits, `sys_axi_in` carries a read of a register with a non-zero generated reset and a write whose value both ports read back.

*Test Coverage*:

* Inbound entry 0 is programmed over SEP_IN with the pass-all recipe the output-fabric tests use, its config word is read back exactly, and all three of its registers are restored to their generated resets with a further config readback at the end.
* The SYS_IN read compares against the generated reset of `CHIP_CONFIG.VERSION_LO`, which is non-zero, so the compare distinguishes a decoded read from a window that answers 0.
* The scratch word written over SYS_IN is read back over SEP_IN and then over SYS_IN, so the same storage is shown reachable from the system port and from SEP_IN; the scratch register is restored to 0 with a readback.
* The access counts are the measured ones, not declarations: the sequence requires its own SEP_IN count to equal exactly the expected number and requires the scoreboard's completed SYS_IN AXI count to equal exactly four.
* The pre-admit SYS_IN read is issued with the error response tolerated and its response is reported, not asserted: the specification leaves the reset state of the sixteen inbound entries to the chiplet integration, so no verdict is claimed on it.
* The inbound filter decides each direction from its own request, with block-by-default when nothing matches, so the write and read channels of the port are driven together: four interleaved passes go out as one outstanding group, SEP_IN seeds the read sources and reads the write targets back, and the filter is observed admitting both directions in the same cycle.
* Every inbound port of this bench carries an agent, so the "unused port tied idle" cell cannot be driven here.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_sys_axi_in_port_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer, with the SYS_IN accesses issued as one-shots on the SYS_IN sequencer: pre-admit read, program and read back entry 0, SYS_IN read of `VERSION_LO`, SYS_IN scratch write, SEP_IN and SYS_IN readbacks, the interleaved write/read group over `sys_axi_in` with its seeds, readbacks and restores under a clocked observer on the filter decision, restore the scratch word and the filter entry, then require both access counts exactly. The leaf requires both SYS_IN read values to have been captured and books a protocol-VIP record carrying the measured SEP_IN and SYS_IN counts against their floors.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SYS-AXI-IN-READ` |After entry 0 admitted the whole address range, a `sys_axi_in` read of `CHIP_CONFIG.VERSION_LO` returned its non-zero generated reset exactly. The pre-admit response at the same address is printed on the same line as reported, not asserted
|`CHK-SYS-AXI-IN-WRITE` |A `sys_axi_in` write of the scratch pattern was read back exactly over SEP_IN and over `sys_axi_in`, and the scoreboard had completed exactly the expected number of SYS_IN and SEP_IN accesses
|`CHK-SYS-AXI-IN-NOT-CLOSED` |A recorded gap, not a compare: the unused-port-tied-idle cell, with the reason -- every inbound port of this bench carries an agent and none is tied to zero. Logged unconditionally
|`CHK-SYS-AXI-IN-PORT` |Ledger line in the leaf: the SYS_IN read value, the scratch readback and the SEP_IN access count, logged after the leaf requires both SYS_IN reads to have been captured. The compares are the sequence's and the scoreboard's
|`CHK-SYS-AXI-IN-DUPLEX` |The interleaved write/read group over `sys_axi_in`: every read returned the word SEP_IN seeded and the write targets held the last pass, and the inbound filter was observed admitting both directions in at least one cycle -- both filter hits set with neither isolate -- so a run whose two channels never overlapped fails
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the admitted `sys_axi_in` read matches the generated reset, the scratch write reads back exactly over both ports, the filter entry and the scratch register are restored, and the measured SEP_IN and SYS_IN access counts equal the expected ones. The pre-admit response and the tied-idle cell are recorded, not claimed. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-194-smc_sep_in_axi_shape_test]]
===== 194 `smc_sep_in_axi_shape_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_sep_in_axi_shape_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (`ocah_axi_vip` AXI4 master), no external VIP

*Objective*: The transfer shapes the 64-to-32-bit conversion chain on the SEP_IN path has to resolve: narrow AxSIZE with byte strobes, a multi-beat INCR burst, and the FIXED and WRAP burst types the path refuses.

*Test Coverage*:

* AxSIZE below the bus width is a narrow transfer whose byte strobes select the lanes. A one-byte write at offset 0 and a two-byte write at offset 2 must each leave the rest of the word alone, so the readback is the merge of the three writes and not any one of them.
* Narrow reads return the addressed lanes of the same word, so the strobe placement is shown on both directions.
* An AxLEN=1 INCR burst is split into consecutive single-beat accesses: the two beats land in two consecutive scratch registers, are read back individually and again as one two-beat burst read, so a beat dropped, merged or reordered fails an exact compare.
* FIXED and WRAP multi-beat bursts: `fabric.adoc` does not state how the SEP_IN path answers a burst type it does not support, so the expectation is the DV-owned one for a refused write -- an AXI error response of either kind, never OKAY and never a wedge -- and both target registers must still hold the values the INCR burst left, so a path that errored the response while still writing is caught. The error code returned is reported, not asserted. Spec gap for the plan owner: a fabric statement fixing the response to an unsupported burst would let this leg hold an exact code.
* Both scratch registers are restored to zero before the sequence ends.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_sep_in_axi_shape_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer: seed a scratch word, overwrite one byte and then one half of it with narrow writes and read the merge back, read the same lanes narrowly, place a two-beat INCR burst across two scratch registers and read it back per register and as one burst, issue the FIXED and the WRAP burst and require an error response with both registers unchanged, then restore both registers. The leaf requires the scoreboard's exact-value compare count to have reached its floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-IN-NARROW-STROBES` |The seeded word, the word after the one-byte write and the word after the two-byte write are exact scoreboard compares, as are the narrow reads of the same lanes; a strobe the fabric ignored or widened fails one of them
|`CHK-SEP-IN-BURST-INCR` |The two beats of the AxLEN=1 INCR write are read back from their own registers and again as one AxLEN=1 read, all exact compares
|`CHK-SEP-IN-BURST-UNSUPPORTED` |The FIXED and the WRAP burst each answered an AXI error response -- the scoreboard fails an OKAY or a timed-out access marked expect-error -- and both registers still read the values the INCR burst left; the error code is reported, not asserted
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every narrow and burst readback matches its exact expectation, the two rejected burst types answer an error response without changing either register, and the scoreboard books at least the expected number of value compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-195-smc_sep_in_axi_flow_control_test]]
===== 195 `smc_sep_in_axi_flow_control_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_sep_in_axi_flow_control_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (`ocah_axi_vip` AXI4 master) with a per-access `AxiTimingProfile` holding the response channels back

*Objective*: With several SEP_IN transactions outstanding and the manager holding BREADY and RREADY low, every access is still answered with the data of the address it was issued for, and the design keeps its response channels stable across the wait.

*Test Coverage*:

* AMBA IHI 0022 A3.2.1: a transfer completes only when VALID and READY are both asserted, VALID may not be withdrawn before that, and the channel's payload stays stable across the wait. B and R are driven by the design, so their stability under the manager's backpressure is the design's obligation and is sampled every cycle the manager holds READY low.
* Three write passes over the eight scratch registers go out as one outstanding group with BREADY held, each pass carrying its own word, so the individual readbacks also show that writes to one address took effect in the order they were issued.
* Four read passes over the same registers go out as one outstanding group with RREADY held, each compared against that address's word, so an access that was dropped, merged, reordered or mis-routed returns the wrong word.
* The stalled-cycle counts are asserted non-zero for all four channels. They are the measurement that the backpressure happened, not the claim: a run whose timing profile stopped reaching the bus fails rather than passing on traffic that never stalled.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_sep_in_axi_flow_control_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer with a clocked observer on the SEP_IN handshake lines: issue the write group under a BREADY hold, read each register back individually, issue the read group under an RREADY hold, restore the registers, then require the observer's violation list to be empty and every channel to have stalled at least once. The leaf requires the scoreboard's exact-value compare count to have reached its floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-IN-FLOW-CONTROL` |Every outstanding write and read completed with the word of the address it named -- the individual readbacks show the last write pass to each address, the read group repeats the same expectation from deeper in the pipeline -- and every one of the four channels was observed stalled at least once
|`CHK-SEP-IN-RESP-STABLE` |Across every cycle in which the manager held BREADY or RREADY low, BVALID and RVALID stayed asserted and BRESP/BID and RDATA/RRESP/RLAST/RID did not change. A withdrawn VALID or a changed payload is recorded and fails the run
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every access in both outstanding groups returns its own address's word, no response channel withdrew VALID or moved its payload while READY was low, all four channels stalled at least once, and the scoreboard books at least the expected number of value compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-196-smc_axil_downstream_concurrency_test]]
===== 196 `smc_axil_downstream_concurrency_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_axil_downstream_concurrency_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (`ocah_axi_vip` AXI4 master); the eFuse-bank window is backed by the eFuse-bank model and the mandatory external window by the reference integration's AXI-Lite error slave

*Objective*: The three downstream AXI-Lite manager ports are independent branches: with accesses to two of the windows outstanding at once, both ports are active in the same cycle and each access is answered by its own window.

*Test Coverage*:

* The two windows answer differently in both response code and data -- the eFuse shim register with OKAY and its generated reset, the mandatory external window with the terminator's DECERR and zero data -- so a crossed route fails on the response code and on the data rather than only on a count.
* The eFuse half is compared by the scoreboard against the generated reset; the external half is required to carry the terminator's zero data, which a neighbouring responder answering in its place would not.
* The cycles in which two of the three activity probes were high together are counted and required to be non-zero, so a group the fabric serialised after all fails instead of passing.
* The SEP_IN monitor attributes a response beat to the last address it saw accepted, which cannot name the transaction while two reads are outstanding; both addresses of the group are registered with it for that reason, and the per-access verdict is the scoreboard's exact expected response code.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_axil_downstream_concurrency_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer with a clocked observer on the three downstream activity probes: issue sixteen eFuse-shim reads alternated with sixteen external-window reads as one outstanding group, then require each external read to carry zero data, each eFuse read to have answered OKAY, and at least one cycle with two ports active together. The leaf requires the scoreboard's exact-value compare count to have reached its floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXIL-DOWNSTREAM-CONCURRENCY` |Every eFuse-shim read answered OKAY with the generated reset and every external-window read answered DECERR with zero data while both accesses were outstanding together, and two downstream manager ports were observed active in the same cycle at least once. The per-probe active-cycle counts are printed on the same line as reported, not asserted
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every access is answered by its own window with that window's response code and data, at least one cycle had two downstream manager ports active together, and the scoreboard books at least the expected number of value compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `axil`.

'''''


[[sec-188-smc_peripheral_instance_decode_test]]
===== 188 `smc_peripheral_instance_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_peripheral_instance_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver; `tb_axil_external_active` sampled on every `clk_smc_i` edge for the PVT wrapper access, which the bench PVT model answers OKAY with zero data

*Objective*: Every peripheral block the generated map declares reads a register with a non-zero generated reset, every multi-instance peripheral is proven at its first and last instance, and the PVT wrapper is proven at the adopter external port.

*Test Coverage*:

* Single-instance blocks are closed by a register whose generated reset is non-zero, so the compare can tell a decoded block from an absent window that reads 0.
* Multi-instance peripherals are additionally proven with distinct patterns written to all probed instances before any is read back, so a stride that aliases two instances returns a neighbour's pattern and fails: the I3C CSR windows, the GPIO interfaces, the mailbox pairs and the log engines at their first and last instance, and the four watchdogs (`CMP` through the `KEY` unlock, one key write per register write), the three I2C controllers (`TARGET_ID`), the four UARTs (`SCR`) and the three telemetry receivers (`INTR_ENABLE`) at every instance. A reset read alone cannot tell N registers from one aliased register or from an undecoded window answering OKAY with the reset word, so every multi-instance block carries a co-resident leg.
* The PVT wrapper is proven at its consumer: the access must drive `smc_external_req_o` while in flight, not merely complete.
* Left open and named, not claimed: the DTP control window (`tb_top` leaves its AXI-Lite response idle, so an access there would never complete) and the bus error units (behind the bit-25 fold of `smc_local_fabric`, so no SEP_IN access reaches one).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_peripheral_instance_decode_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer: sweep the I3C, GPIO, mailbox, watchdog and log-engine instances, then the single-instance blocks with the I2C, UART and telemetry co-resident legs, then route the PVT access; name the two unreachable cells, require the exact-value compare count and the access count to have reached their floors, and print the cell ledger. The leaf re-checks the scoreboard compare count and books a protocol-VIP record.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PERIPHERAL-INSTANCE` |The cell ledger: one line per block or instance cell closed with the measured evidence behind it, plus one line per cell left NOT closed in this bench with the tie-off or address fold that prevents it. The NOT-closed lines are recorded gaps, not compares
|`CHK-PERIPHERAL-INSTANCE-DECODE` |Summary line: cells closed, the scoreboard exact-value compare count against its floor, the access count, the PVT external-port active cycles and the number of cells left open. Logged after the per-block compares and both floors have held
|`CHK-PERIPHERAL-INSTANCE-FLOOR` |The leaf requires the scoreboard's `sys_axi_value_checks_seen` to have reached `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` before this token is logged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every probed block matches its non-zero generated reset, every probed instance returns its own co-resident pattern, the PVT access drives the external port, and the scoreboard books at least `EXPECTED_VALUE_CHECKS` compares. The DTP window and the bus error units are recorded as not closed. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

==== RESET

[[sec-038-smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test]]
===== 38 `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: LIVE/CONNECTIVITY DFT test_en_i bypass + Zeroer reset-override.

*Test Coverage*:

* DV-CARD: SMCCGP0_003 ANCHOR: smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DFT-BYPASS-FREE-RUN` |Named compare in `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-NONVAC` |Non-vacuity count in `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test_seq.py`
|`CHK-RESET-OVERRIDE-FREE-RUN` |Named compare in `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DFT-BYPASS-FREE-RUN`, `CHK-NONVAC`, `CHK-RESET-OVERRIDE-FREE-RUN` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `clock`.

'''''

[[sec-039-smc_reset_unit_lock_test]]
===== 39 `smc_reset_unit_lock_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_reset_unit_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK and SS_CONFIG_LOCK are write-one-to-set and mask the registers they guard.

*Test Coverage*:

* `RESET_UNIT.SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK`, which no other cocotb testcase reads; `fw/tests/cold_reset_lock_sanity` exercises the same two properties from firmware, and this testcase covers them without a firmware dependency.
* `RESET_UNIT.SS_CONFIG_LOCK`, which no other testcase names or drives.
* `reset_unit.rdl:87-95` and `:18-26` declare both lock fields as `sw = rw; hw = r; onwrite = woset;` reset 0, so a taken lock cannot be released by software.
* `smc_subsystem_resets.sv` gates each target with the inverted lock: `SS_COLD_RESET_N` with `~ss_cold_reset_lock`, and `SS_CONFIG` at `:81` with `config_filtered_wr_mask = (~ss_config_lock) & ss_config_wr_mask`. `cool_reset_isolate_cfg` records the cold-reset half from the other side, as its reason for *not* comparing that register, so the masking is asserted here and nowhere else.
* One testcase rather than two: the registers have the same declaration and the same gate, so a second would differ only in two addresses. The three checkers run once per pair and are tagged with it, and the sequence requires all six tokens, so a pair silently dropped from the list fails rather than shrinking the test.

*Test Procedure*:

The scenario has two implementations that share this claim: the cocotb
`smc_reset_unit_lock_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM `smc_reset_unit_lock_test_seq.svh`
(`--framework uvm`). For each of the two (target, lock) pairs:

[arabic]
. Require both bits under test to be clear in the lock, since `woset` means a set bit could not have been cleared by this sequence. (cocotb only. The SV-UVM sequence performs no lock pre-read: it locks a fresh bit per pass and expects the accumulated `bits 1..1+loop_index` back, so from pass 1 onward a lock that ignored writes or reset to the wrong value fails on the accumulation instead.)
. Write bit 0 to the target with nothing locked and read it back, proving the register is writable before any lock exists.
. Write bit 1 to the lock and require it to set, then write all zeroes and require it to remain set.
. Write bits 0 and 1 to the target in one access. Require bit 1 to stay clear and bit 0 to follow the write.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-RESET-LOCK-ARM[<pair>]` |The target accepts a write with the lock clear, so "unchanged" later cannot be explained by a register that never changes
|`CHK-RESET-LOCK-WOSET[<pair>]` |A set lock bit survives an all-zeroes write, per `onwrite = woset`
|`CHK-RESET-LOCK-MASKS-WRITE[<pair>]` |In one write carrying both bits, the locked bit does not follow software while the unlocked bit does -- so the write demonstrably reached the register
|`CHK-LOCK-TARGET-WRITABLE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): positive control: the target accepts a write at this pass's bit while the lock is still clear, so "unchanged" later cannot be satisfied by a register that never changes
|`CHK-LOCK-SET` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the written bit appears in the lock register, which accumulates the bits earlier passes locked
|`CHK-LOCK-STICKY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): an all-zeroes write does not release any set lock bit, per `onwrite = woset`
|`CHK-LOCK-MASKS-TARGET` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): after the lock is taken, a write clearing the locked bit leaves it set -- the lock filters the write bit-enables before they reach the storage flop (`smc_subsystem_resets.sv:81`, `:100`)
|`CHK-LOCK-FREE-BIT-MOVED` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the same write carries never-locked bit 0, and that bit is required to change in both directions -- so "unchanged" cannot be explained by the write being dropped
|`CHK-CSR-RESP` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): every SEP_IN CSR access completes with an OKAY response
|`CHK-FUSE-SENSE-DONE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): warm-reset domain release after fuse sense completes
|`CHK-NONVAC` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): reconciles the accesses the body issued against its own loop bounds -- a control-flow invariant that catches a truncated pass, not a DUT-failable compare. `CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` is the observed-side counterpart
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the `lock_csr` feature's comparison count, taken from the always-on scoreboard fed by the PASSIVE monitor, is at or above the floor the completed passes imply (five predicted reads per lock pair per pass)
|`CHK-SB-LOCK-CSR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): always-on scoreboard, feature `lock_csr`: `smc_lock_csr_ref_model` applies `woset` to the lock and lock-filtered bit-enables to the guarded register, rebuilt from the traffic the passive monitor saw rather than from the sequence's own bookkeeping. Note the asymmetry in where the two rules come from: `woset` is specification-sourced (`reset_unit.rdl:18-26`, `:87-95`), but the bit-enable filtering is transcribed from the RTL it checks (`smc_subsystem_resets.sv:81`, `:100`), so on that half the model would be wrong in the same direction as a wrong design. It corroborates; the primary proof of masking is the sequence-side `CHK-LOCK-MASKS-TARGET`, whose expectation comes from the bench's own accumulation
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all six checkers hold, three per pair: each lock holds 0x2 through an all-zeroes write, and a 0x3 write to its target reads back 0x1. This testcase leaves one bit set in each lock for the rest of its simulation, which is what `woset` means; each testcase runs its own simulation.

A taken lock is irreversible for the rest of the simulation it was taken in, which is what
`woset` means. The two implementations of this scenario handle that differently, and the
difference is part of the claim:

* the cocotb testcase owns its simulation and locks one bit;
* the SV-UVM testcase runs at least 16 scenario passes back to back in ONE simulation with no
  reset between them (`ocah_test.MinDefaultLoops`), so each pass locks a FRESH bit --
  bit 1 + `loop_index` -- while bit 0 is never locked in any pass and stays the writability
  control. Measured PASS on VCS at seed 1: by the sixteenth pass each lock held 0x1FFFE, and a
  write of 0x00000001 to its target read back 0x0001FFFF. A loop knob that would run past the
  lockable bits fails the run rather than aliasing silently.

Under `--framework uvm` -- a framework no run in this package executes, so the SV-UVM rows above are not closed by any run here -- the governing checker set is the SV-UVM one in the table above -- the cocotb ids named at the head of this Pass Criteria do not run there -- and it holds instead, and
`+SMC_LOCK_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.

'''''

[[sec-040-smc_cold_reset_repeated_test]]
===== 40 `smc_cold_reset_repeated_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cold_reset_repeated_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS repeated cold-reset re-assert scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* assert half: a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on the asserted levels. It doubles as the stimulus hold -- `smc_reset_ctrl` de-glitches `rst_cold_ni` / `rst_cool_ni` over 32 `clk_ref_i` samples, so a fixed hold shorter than that window is silently rejected and resets nothing. Holding until the reset is *observed* cannot be short.
* hold half: after the wait matched, the pin stays low across a further `MID_ASSERT_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` `clk_ref_i` edges and EVERY one of those samples must still read the asserted levels. This is a separate observation in time from the wait (the wait's match and a RAW_SAMPLE dispatched straight after it land in the same delta region, so such a snapshot could not fail unless the wait already had -- [NO-ALWAYS-PASS-CHECKER]). A DUT that releases the cold/cool path early, anywhere inside the window, fails here.
* release half: a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on the released levels followed by the post-release `SAMPLE` (the checked_cleared leg). No fixed `ClockCycles` wait stands in for completion on the reassert / between / recover path ([NO-BLIND-DELAY-SYNC] / [TIMEOUT-MUST-FAIL]).
* `skip_mem_repair_o` against the isolate-request pin: the pad is the one the DV-owned pad table (`doc/integrator/meta/ocah_gpio_table.csv`) names "Isolate Request", and `clk_rst.adoc` ("Function Level Reset", "Memory Test Bypass") defines pin-based isolation as that pin gated by `ISOLATE_REQ_PINEN_REG` and makes it an isolation source that activates the bypass. The testcase module first programs `ISOLATE_REQ_PINEN_REG` to all ones over SEP_IN and reads it back (`smc_isolate_req_pinen_seq`); then, with no FLR signalled and `ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG` at its reset 0, the output must read 0 with the pin driven low and 1 with it driven high, each within a bounded poll (`CHK-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR-PIN`), and 0 again when fuse sense completes after the last reset with the pin held low (`CHK-SENSE-DONE-REPAIR-ENABLED`; the resets have returned the enable to 0, and a low pin is no isolation source under either enable value). The level the output shows with the pad undriven is reported, not asserted. Observation for the pad-table owner: the table recommends a pull-down for this pad, while the bench pad model reads it as 1 when undriven; the sequence drives the pin and does not rely on the undriven level.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cold_reset_repeated_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` |Every `clk_ref_i` sample across the `MID_ASSERT_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` window after the assert handshake matched carries the asserted levels (`_hold_raw` in `smc_reset_seq_base.py`); each sample carries the expectation, so the scoreboard fails the run at the first sample that leaves them, one token per held window
|`CHK-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR-PIN` |`skip_mem_repair_o` 0 within a bounded poll of driving the isolate-request pad low and 1 within a bounded poll of driving it high, with no FLR signalled and `ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG` at 0; expiry or a wrong level fails
|`CHK-SENSE-DONE-REPAIR-ENABLED` |`tb_fuse_sense_done` rises within a bounded wait after the last reset release and `skip_mem_repair_o` reads 0 at that point with the pin held low; expiry or a 1 fails
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD`, `CHK-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR-PIN` and `CHK-SENSE-DONE-REPAIR-ENABLED` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.

'''''

[[sec-041-smc_cold_reset_test]]
===== 41 `smc_cold_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cold_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS cold-reset / POR / cool SMC_001 scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* every reset step is a bounded `WAIT_STATE` handshake carrying the exact expected levels, so the scoreboard owns the verdict and expiry raises with the last observed state ([TIMEOUT-MUST-FAIL]). No fixed settle sits on the proof path -- recovery is a wait for the released levels, not a delay ([NO-BLIND-DELAY-SYNC]).
* the POR/cold interaction fence (S7) is a *hold* monitor, not a first-match poll: with `powergood_stable_o==0` every sample across a window wider than the DUT's 32-sample cold de-glitch must still show the cold-stable and primary resets asserted. A delayed incorrect release inside the window fails, which a return-on-first-match poll could not see ([NO-ALWAYS-PASS-CHECKER]).
* the non-vacuity fence (S9) counts the scoreboard's *fail-capable* reset checks (`reset_samples_seen` + `reset_raw_checks_seen` + `reset_wait_checks_seen`) at every step boundary and requires each of S3..S8 to have advanced that total **with checks of its own**: the shared `_recover_sample()` tail's contribution is tracked separately and subtracted, so a step is not credited for the recovery every step ends with and dropping that step's own assert leg fails the fence. Expectation-free `RAW_SAMPLE` observations are booked in a separate counter and cannot satisfy it either, so a step whose expectations were dropped (or which never ran) fails instead of being papered over ([NO-ZERO-ACTIVITY-PASS] / [NO-DUMMY-DEAD-CODE]).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cold_reset_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BRINGUP-LEVELS` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-COLD-ASSERT-PRIMARY` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-COOL-PRIMARY` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-INT-COOL-SEQ` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-INT-POR-COLD` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` |The cold-reset levels hold at every `clk_ref_i` sample of the `POR_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` window while `powergood_i` is low (`_hold_raw` in `smc_reset_seq_base.py`); the scoreboard fails the run at the first sample that leaves them
|`CHK-NONVAC` |Non-vacuity count in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`
|`CHK-POWERGOOD-GATES` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |Named compare in `smc_cold_reset_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-BRINGUP-LEVELS`, `CHK-COLD-ASSERT-PRIMARY`, `CHK-COOL-PRIMARY`, `CHK-INT-COOL-SEQ`, `CHK-INT-POR-COLD`, `CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` (and 3 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`, `smoke`.
'''''

[[sec-042-smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test]]
===== 42 `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: FLR pin → skip_mem_repair / rst_cool_no / isolate_req_o.

*Test Coverage*:

* cfg_flr_pf_active_i cool reset.
* No Force; not rst_cool_ni.
* BMC/primary-chiplet not claimed.
* `skip_mem_repair_o` is a compared output, not a printed one. `clk_rst.adoc` ("Memory Test Bypass") activates it automatically when either FLR-triggered or pin-based isolation is asserted, so the isolate-request pad is driven low for the whole sequence and the output is required to read 0 with nothing isolating (`CHK-FLR-IDLE`), 1 within a bounded poll once the FLR-latched request drives every `isolate_req_o` bit through `ISOLATE_REQ_SMCEN_REG`, and 0 again within a bounded poll after software clears `ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG` (`CHK-FLR-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR`). Poll expiry fails.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FLR-BASIC` |Roll-up token logged after the zero-count, cool, isolate and skip-mem-repair legs have each held their own raise-capable compares; it carries no compare of its own
|`CHK-FLR-COOL` |Named compare in `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-FLR-IDLE` |ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG reads 0, `isolate_req_o` is 0, `rst_cool_from_flr` is released and `skip_mem_repair_o` is 0 with the isolate pin low and no FLR signalled; each is an assert
|`CHK-FLR-ISO` |Named compare in `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-FLR-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR` |`skip_mem_repair_o` reaches 1 within a bounded poll while the FLR-latched request drives `isolate_req_o`, and 0 within a bounded poll after the request is cleared; expiry fails
|`CHK-FLR-WARM` |Named compare in `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-FLR-ZERO-CNT` |Named compare in `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-FLR-BASIC`, `CHK-FLR-COOL`, `CHK-FLR-IDLE`, `CHK-FLR-ISO`, `CHK-FLR-SKIP-MEM-REPAIR`, `CHK-FLR-WARM`, `CHK-FLR-ZERO-CNT` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.

'''''

[[sec-045-smc_flr_sanity_test]]
===== 45 `smc_flr_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_flr_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS FLR post-release sanity scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* mid-assert: a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on `rst_primary_ref/smc == 0` while the cold-stable path stays released (`clk_rst.adoc`: cool reset is a primary-level reset). The wait also bounds the stimulus from below -- `smc_reset_ctrl` de-glitches `rst_cool_ni` over 32 `clk_ref_i` samples, so a fixed hold shorter than that window is silently rejected and no reset is taken. Holding the pin low until the reset is *observed* cannot be too short.
* post-release: `SCRATCH_COLD_WARM_0` is read *before* being rewritten and must equal its mapped reset value, not the pattern written before the pulse -- cool reaches the warm reset domain this register lives in. The following write/read-back is the positive control that the CSR path is alive again.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_flr_sanity_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FLR-COOL-CSR-RECOVERY` |A `rst_cool_ni` pulse with `cfg_flr_pf_active_i` inactive drives the primary resets low and back within their bounds, `SCRATCH_COLD_WARM_0` then reads its reset value in place of the pre-cool pattern and takes a fresh write/read-back; an unobserved reset, a surviving pattern or a dead CSR path fails
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-FLR-COOL-CSR-RECOVERY` holds after its compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-047-smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test]]
===== 47 `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: SMC OSS PyUVM IRQ-during-powergood-glitch test.

*Test Coverage*:

* **the glitch is proven to have happened** -- a bounded `WAIT_STATE` plus an exact mid-glitch `RAW_SAMPLE` (`powergood_stable_o==0` with the cold-stable and both primary resets asserted) ride on reset items, so the scoreboard owns the verdict. A DUT that ignores `powergood_i` fails here instead of quietly turning the whole test into a no-op ([NO-ALWAYS-PASS-CHECKER]).
* **the window is observed continuously** -- a cycle-accurate `clk_ref_i` watcher runs from before the glitch until after recovery and fails on the first resolvable non-zero (or on an unresolvable) IRQ aggregate. Bookend samples alone could not catch a transient assert that is gone again by the final sample.
* **mid-window SAMPLEs are dispatched** during the glitch hold and while recovery is in flight, so the scoreboard books checked (not observed-only) IRQ evidence from inside the window as well.
* **recovery is proven, not assumed** -- the recovered idle-IRQ leg is a negative check, so it is preceded by a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on all five reset observables released. Without it, a DUT stuck in reset presents the same all-zero IRQ aggregates and passes identically.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-IRQ-PG-GLITCH-NO-SPURIOUS` |Named compare in `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-IRQ-PG-GLITCH-NO-SPURIOUS` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `combined`, `irq`.
'''''

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-JTAG-RST-BASIC`, `CHK-JTAG-RST-COOL`, `CHK-JTAG-RST-IDLE`, `CHK-JTAG-RST-SS0`, `CHK-JTAG-RST-WARM-SCRATCH` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.
'''''

[[sec-050-smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test]]
===== 50 `smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI), ocah_jtag_vip

*Objective*: Run JTAG-adjacent CSR health checks across an observed cool reset, plus CPU JTAG pin checks.

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG-adjacent reset recovery proxy sequence.
* DEFENDS: rst_cool_ni is held low until the reset is observed taken (rst_primary_ref_clk_n and rst_primary_smc_clk_n both 0 while powergood and cold stay 1), then released and the release observed; the cold-domain SCRATCH_COLD_2 pattern written beforehand is cleared to its generated reset; CHIP_CONFIG_VERSION_LO reads its generated reset value on both sides.
* DOES NOT DEFEND: any JTAG TAP protocol behaviour beyond the CPU JTAG pin checks; the reset here is a cool-reset proxy, not a JTAG-initiated reset.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-JTAG-DTMCS` |Named compare in `smc_jtag_vip_utils.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CPU-JTAG-IDCODE` |Named compare in `smc_jtag_vip_utils.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CPU-JTAG-SCAN-ACTIVITY` |Named compare in `smc_jtag_vip_utils.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`cool_asserted` |Bounded WAIT_STATE: reset observed taken while held; expiry fails with the last observed state
|`cool_released` |Bounded WAIT_STATE: reset observed released; expiry fails with the last observed state
|scoreboard |SCRATCH_COLD_2_CLEARED reads the generated reset, so the cool reset was taken and cleared the pattern written before it
|scoreboard |SCRATCH_COLD_2_REWRITE reads back the pattern, so the register is writable again after the reset
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-CPU-JTAG-DTMCS`, `CHK-CPU-JTAG-IDCODE`, `CHK-CPU-JTAG-SCAN-ACTIVITY` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-052-smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test]]
===== 52 `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Run CSR access/persistence checks around a public cool reset pulse.

*Test Coverage*:

* CSR behaviour across the public cool-reset control.
* Addresses and expected defaults both come from the generated PeakRDL map via `smc_csr_field_catalog` (which also cross-checks the address that is actually driven against the cataloged symbol).
* Reset synchronization is handshake-based, never a fixed settle: `smc_reset_ctrl` de-glitches `rst_cool_ni` over `RESET_DEGLITCH_WIDTH` `clk_ref_i` samples, so the pin is held low until the DUT is *observed* to enter cool reset (`rst_warm_smc_clk_n` / `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` drop), and released only until the same observables are back and the fuse-sense/warm release pipe has completed. Measured on VCS at seed 1: `rst_primary_smc_clk_n` fell 94 smc clocks after the pin, and `rst_warm_smc_clk_n` came back 2346 smc clocks after release -- a fixed delay short of either would prove nothing.
* Reset topology, taken from the specification and the register map rather than the RTL: `clk_rst.adoc` (SMC Reset Sources, Primary Reset Activation Sources) lists the cool reset as a Primary Reset activation source, and Primary Reset covers "SMC control and configuration registers"; `misc_wrap.rdl` instantiates the two scratch windows as `scratch_cold` and `scratch_cold_warm`, naming the reset domains each belongs to. A cool pulse therefore returns `SCRATCH_COLD_WARM[1]` to its RDL reset, which is what `CHK-COOL-RESET-CLEARS-WARM-SCRATCH` compares. Which register a warm-only reset would leave intact is not claimed here.
* NOT claimed: either scratch window surviving a reset the other one takes. No stimulus here drops `rst_warm_ni` without also dropping `rst_primary_ni`, so separating the two domains needs a warm-only source (the `SS_WARM_RESET_N` control or the SEP WDT pin) and is left to a testcase that has one.
* Both waits are bounded and fail on expiry.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq`. The scenario has two implementations that
share this claim: the cocotb `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM
`smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.svh` (`--framework uvm`). The SV-UVM view needs no
reset agent -- `smc_tb_if` carries the `rst_cool_n` pin and the reset-unit observables, so the
sequence drives and polls them directly. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-COOL-RESET-ASSERTED` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-COOL-RESET-CLEARS-WARM-SCRATCH` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-COOL-RESET-RELEASED` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CSR-BASELINE-RO` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CSR-POST-COOL-RECOVERY` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CSR-PRE-COOL-READBACK` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CSR-WRITABLE-AFTER-COOL` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MULTI-RESET-CSR-SWEEP` |Named compare in `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CSR-PRE-COOL-READBACK` |SV-UVM: both scratch windows hold a NON-ZERO pass-specific pattern before the pulse. Required non-zero, because "reads 0 after the reset" says nothing if the register held 0 going in
|`CHK-COOL-RESET-ASSERTED` |SV-UVM: the pin pulse survives the de-glitch and both `rst_primary_smc_clk_n` and `rst_warm_smc_clk_n` are polled down -- not waited out with a fixed delay
|`CHK-COOL-RESET-RELEASED` |SV-UVM: both observables are polled back up after the pin is released
|`CHK-COOL-CLEARS-SCRATCH` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): both `SCRATCH_COLD[1]` and `SCRATCH_COLD_WARM[1]` read their reset value afterwards, which is the reset topology cited under Test Coverage
|`CHK-CSR-PATH-RECOVERED` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the read path returns real content, not just OKAY -- `CHIP_CONFIG.VERSION_LO` reads its non-zero generated default (0x000100A0) again, so a path that came back dead or stuck at zero fails
|`CHK-CSR-RW-RECOVERED` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): a fresh write and readback lands after the reset, and the restore leaves the register at its reset value
|`CHK-CSR-RESP` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): every SEP_IN CSR access completes with an OKAY response
|`CHK-FUSE-SENSE-DONE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): warm-reset domain release after fuse sense completes, checked before the pulse and again after it
|`CHK-NONVAC` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): reconciles the accesses the body issued against its own loop bounds -- a control-flow invariant that catches a truncated pass, not a DUT-failable compare. `CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` is the observed-side counterpart
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the `scratch_csr` feature's comparison count, taken from the always-on scoreboard fed by the PASSIVE monitor, is at or above the floor the completed passes imply (six predicted scratch reads per pass)
|`CHK-SB-SCRATCH-CSR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): always-on scoreboard, feature `scratch_csr`: `smc_scratch_csr_ref_model` re-baselines on the same cool reset through `smc_csr_reset_epoch`, so a predictor that ignored the cool reset fails here
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-COOL-RESET-ASSERTED`, `CHK-COOL-RESET-CLEARS-WARM-SCRATCH`, `CHK-COOL-RESET-RELEASED`, `CHK-CSR-BASELINE-RO`, `CHK-CSR-POST-COOL-RECOVERY`, `CHK-CSR-PRE-COOL-READBACK` (and 2 more) hold after their compares.

Under `--framework uvm` -- a framework no run in this package executes, so the SV-UVM rows above are not closed by any run here -- the governing checker set is the SV-UVM one in the table above -- the cocotb ids named at the head of this Pass Criteria do not run there -- and it holds instead, and
`+SMC_CSR_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run. Each of the 16 scenario passes drives its own
complete write / pulse / check / restore cycle with a fresh pattern, so back-to-back recovery is
re-proved rather than assumed. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-054-smc_powergood_glitch_test]]
===== 54 `smc_powergood_glitch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_powergood_glitch_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS powergood-glitch recovery scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* mid-glitch (`clk_rst.adoc` "Primary Reset Activation Sources": POR "holds SMC functional reset gated until power-good is stable"): with `powergood_stable_o==0` the cold-stable and both primary resets must read asserted -- and at **every** sample of the glitch, not at one instant, since a single snapshot also passes for a DUT that releases the functional reset path anywhere else inside the same window ([EXACT-EXPECTATION]). Those expectations ride on the reset items, so the scoreboard -- not this sequence -- owns the verdict, and a DUT that ignores `powergood_i` fails instead of producing an OBSERVED-ONLY snapshot.
* recovery: a `WAIT_STATE` handshake on the released levels, not a fixed settle, so recovery latency is bounded and expiry raises with the last observed state ([NO-BLIND-DELAY-SYNC] / [TIMEOUT-MUST-FAIL]).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_powergood_glitch_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` |Every `clk_ref_i` sample across the `MID_ASSERT_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` window after the assert handshake matched carries the asserted levels (`_hold_raw` in `smc_reset_seq_base.py`); each sample carries the expectation, so the scoreboard fails the run at the first sample that leaves them, one token for the glitch window
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` holds after its hold window and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.

'''''

[[sec-055-smc_reset_recovery_matrix_test]]
===== 55 `smc_reset_recovery_matrix_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_reset_recovery_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS canonical reset/powergood recovery matrix.

*Test Coverage*:

* the assert half rides an exact expectation on the reset item, so a DUT that never asserts the driven reset fails there instead of leaving an OBSERVED-ONLY snapshot behind ([NO-ALWAYS-PASS-CHECKER]). The expected values come from `hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (POR holds the functional reset gated until power-good is stable; cool reset is a primary-level reset, so it asserts `rst_primary_*` while the cold-stable path stays released).
* the "still asserted while the pin/level is held" half is a *hold*, not a single sample: all `MID_ASSERT_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` samples of the driven window must carry the asserted levels. One instantaneous snapshot passes for a DUT that releases the driven reset at any other instant of the same window, so each of the three legs checks the whole window instead ([EXACT-EXPECTATION]).
* the recovery half is a bounded `WAIT_STATE` handshake on the released levels, not a fixed settle: expiry raises with the last observed state ([NO-BLIND-DELAY-SYNC] / [TIMEOUT-MUST-FAIL]).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_reset_recovery_matrix_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` |Every `clk_ref_i` sample across the `MID_ASSERT_HOLD_REF_CYCLES` window after the assert handshake matched carries the asserted levels (`_hold_raw` in `smc_reset_seq_base.py`); each sample carries the expectation, so the scoreboard fails the run at the first sample that leaves them, one token per leg of the matrix
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-MID-ASSERT-HOLD` holds after its hold window and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `reset`.
'''''
