[[sec-183-smc_cold_boot_chain_order_test]]
===== 183 `smc_cold_boot_chain_order_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cold_boot_chain_order_test`

*BFM / Model*: reset agent driving `powergood_i` and `rst_cold_ni` (no external VIP); the boundary observables and the hart-0 retire trace sampled on every `clk_smc_i` edge in `tb_top`

*Objective*: On a second cold reset, power-good is stable before fuse sense completes, fuse sense completes before the core reset releases, the first retired instruction sits at the specified ROM vector, and cluster isolation is held past both `smc_init_mem_done_o` and the core reset release.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every observable -- `powergood_stable_o`, `smc_fuse_sense_done_o`, `smc_init_mem_done_o`, core 0 `tb_cpu_core_reset_n`, cluster `tb_cpu_cluster_isolate` -- is first required at its reset level while the cold pin is held, within a bound. Each recorded rise is therefore a bring-up event of this reset and not a stale level left from the initial bring-up.
* All stamps come from one `clk_smc_i` timeline taken by a single monitor, and the ordering claims are strict inequalities between those stamps; each observable is required resolvable at every sample.
* The first retired PC is compared against the vector `rom.adoc` pins, not against the `CPU_CTRL` reset value the DUT reads back.
* The two bounded reset legs are scoreboard `WAIT_STATE` handshakes carrying exact expected levels, and the leaf requires the scoreboard to have booked at least two of them, so an expired bound raises there with the last observed state.
* What this bench cannot show is named and left open: the automatic SRAM zeroing never runs (`smc_disable_sram_auto_init_i` is tied high in `tb_top`, so `smc_init_mem_done_o` rises as soon as the cluster reset releases), the repair and MBIST done inputs are tied high so there is no engine to observe, and the uncore reset has no separate probe.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sample the entry state, drop `powergood_i` and assert `rst_cold_ni`, and match a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on power-good low with the cold-stable and both primary resets asserted and left stable.
. Require every boot observable to reach its reset level while the pin is held.
. Start the per-edge monitor, restore `powergood_i`, hold the cold pin ten `clk_ref_i` cycles, release it, and match the bounded released handshake.
. Wait, bounded, for every observable to reach its boot level and for hart 0 to retire its first instruction.
. Assert the ordering inequalities and the ROM vector, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-COLD-BOOT-ORDER` |`powergood_stable` < `fuse_sense_done` < `core_reset_release` <= first retired instruction, as strict inequalities between cycle stamps on one `clk_smc_i` timeline; any observable that never reached its boot level inside the bound raises first
|`CHK-COLD-BOOT-FIRST-FETCH-AT-ROM-VECTOR` |The first retired hart-0 PC equals the cold-reset ROM vector `rom.adoc` pins, and the retire happened no earlier than the core reset release
|`CHK-CLUSTER-SELF-ISOLATED-AT-COLD-BOOT` |`tb_cpu_cluster_isolate` read 1 while the cold pin was held and released strictly after both `smc_init_mem_done_o` rose and the core reset released
|`CHK-COLD-BOOT-NOT-CLOSED` |A recorded gap, not a compare: sram-auto-init-runs, repair-triggered-by-fuse-sense-done and the repair/MBIST steps, and release-requires-uncore-out-of-reset, each with the tie-off or missing probe that prevents it. Logged unconditionally
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |One line per bounded reset leg the run took, naming the bound and the `clk_ref_i` cycle at which the expected levels matched. The compare is the scoreboard's -- an expired bound raises there -- so these lines are the record of the bounded legs, not a separate compare
|`CHK-COLD-BOOT-CHAIN` |Ledger line in the leaf: the stamp dictionary, the first-fetch cycle and PC, and the scoreboard's reset `WAIT_STATE` count. Logged after the leaf requires at least two booked wait checks and requires the sequence to have observed both the reset levels and a first fetch
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every boot observable reaches its reset level and then its boot level inside the bounds, the three ordering inequalities and the two isolation inequalities hold, the first retired PC is the ROM vector, and the scoreboard books at least two reset `WAIT_STATE` checks. The memory-repair and SRAM-init steps are recorded as not closed and are not part of the claim. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-189-smc_primary_reset_scope_test]]
===== 189 `smc_primary_reset_scope_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_primary_reset_scope_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver for the CSR legs, with the reset items dispatched to the reset agent through a one-shot; `tb_cpu_core_reset_n`, `tb_cpu_rom_read_count`, the SEP_IN request and response channels and the UART0 TX pad output enable sampled every clock edge of the held window

*Objective*: With a core fetching ROM and UART0 mid-frame, the cool pin asserts the primary reset and the cores and the peripheral controllers are held across the whole asserted window; an outbound filter register holding a pattern before the reset reads its generated reset after it, which is how far the fabric half is taken in this bench.

*Test Coverage*:

* Each consumer is first put into a state a reset would visibly end -- the ROM read counter advancing, a pattern resident in `OUTBOUND_FILTER_CTRL[0].END_ADDR`, UART0 enabled and driving a start bit -- so the held observation is not trivially satisfied by an idle DUT.
* Every held claim is a per-sample compare across the window rather than one instant: the core reset must read 0 at every sample and the UART TX pad output enable must read 0 at every sample after a stated settle. A consumer released anywhere inside the window fails.
* The fabric is **not** claimed held, and the four SEP_IN tallies the run records say why rather than standing in as a check. `arready`/`awready` read 1 just before the cool pin drops and 1 at every sample of the window, so ready is not withdrawn under primary reset. `arvalid` reads 0 at every sample because the SEP_IN AXI master takes `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` as its own reset and parks its request lines there, so no request can be presented inside the window and a no-response would be the bench's reset rather than the DUT's refusal. `rvalid`/`bvalid` read 0 for the same reason the window is idle -- on any RTL. None of the four is asserted on.
* The pad *value* probe cannot carry the peripheral claim: `tb_uart0_tx_from_dut` mirrors `core2pad_o`, which a GPIO wrap in reset parks at 0 with its driver off. The pad's output enable is the observable that distinguishes "transmitter stopped driving" from "wrap parked the pin".
* A ROM fetch counted inside the window must sit at its head, before the cores' reset had propagated; an advance later in the window fails as a fetch by a core that was not held.
* After release, the outbound filter register, UART0 `CTRL` and `CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL` are required to read their generated resets, and core 0's reset release is bounded.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sample the entry state; prove the cores are fetching by measuring `tb_cpu_rom_read_count` advancing over a window.
. Write and read back the filter pattern; arm UART0 mid-frame at the slowest divisor so the de-glitched reset assertion lands inside the frame.
. Assert `rst_cool_ni` and match a bounded `WAIT_STATE` on power-good and cold-stable released with both primary resets asserted and left stable.
. Sample the held window every clock edge and assert the two per-sample held claims plus the frozen ROM counter, recording the four SEP_IN tallies.
. Release the pin, match the bounded released handshake, bound core 0's reset release, and read the three registers against their generated resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PRIMARY-RESET-CORES-HELD` |`tb_cpu_core_reset_n` read 0 at every sample of the held window, the ROM read counter stopped advancing after the reset propagated, and core 0 released inside its bound after `rst_cool_ni` returned. The pre-reset fetch count is reported as the precondition
|`CHK-PRIMARY-RESET-FABRIC-REVERTED` |The outbound filter register went from the resident pattern to its generated reset across the reset and answered a read again afterwards, so the primary reset reached the fabric configuration registers. The line also carries the four recorded SEP_IN tallies and states that held-state is not claimed and why
|`CHK-PRIMARY-RESET-PERIPHERALS-HELD` |UART0 was mid-frame with its TX pad output enable asserted when the reset landed; the output enable read 0 at every sample after the stated settle, and UART0 `CTRL` and `CLOCK_GATE_CONTROL` read their generated resets afterwards
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |One line per bounded reset leg the run took, naming the bound and the `clk_ref_i` cycle at which the expected levels matched. The compare is the scoreboard's -- an expired bound raises there -- so these lines are the record of the bounded legs, not a separate compare
|`CHK-PRIMARY-RESET-SCOPE` |Ledger line in the leaf: the held-window sample count, the two per-sample held tallies and the four recorded SEP_IN tallies. Logged after the leaf requires at least two booked reset `WAIT_STATE` checks, the SEP_IN exact-value compare floor, a non-empty held window and a non-zero pre-reset fetch delta
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both per-sample held claims hold across the whole window, the ROM counter freezes at its head, the three registers read their generated resets after release, core 0 releases inside its bound, and the scoreboard books at least two reset `WAIT_STATE` checks and the SEP_IN value-compare floor. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-191-smc_reset_sync_edge_test]]
===== 191 `smc_reset_sync_edge_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_reset_sync_edge_test`

*BFM / Model*: reset agent for the bounded handshakes, with `rst_cool_ni` driven directly at a controlled sub-cycle phase; `clk_ref_i` and `clk_smc_i` rising-edge timestamps and both synchronized reset outputs read at simulation-time resolution

*Objective*: Assertion of the synchronized primary resets is asynchronous and anchored on `clk_ref_i`, deassertion is synchronous to each output's own target clock, and more than one target-clock edge separates the release anchor from each output's rise.

*Test Coverage*:

* The pin is driven one third into a clock period on both the assert and the release, so none of the timestamp coincidences below can be an artefact of the stimulus landing on a clock edge.
* Assertion: `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` is required to read 0 at the very instant `rst_primary_ref_clk_no` falls, and that instant is required to equal the most recent recorded `clk_ref_i` rising edge. A synchronous assertion in the SMC domain would have waited for the next `clk_smc_i` edge and could not coincide.
* Deassertion: each output's rise is required to fall exactly on a recorded rising edge of its own clock, and the reference output's rise is required to be a whole number of `clk_ref_i` periods after the release anchor -- the first `clk_ref_i` edge after the pin was released.
* Stages: `clk_rst.adoc` pins no stage count, so only "at least two target-clock edges" is asserted, in both domains, and the measured counts are reported.
* The cool pin is the source because its de-glitch path has no release extender. Both bounded legs are scoreboard `WAIT_STATE` handshakes with exact expected levels, and the leaf requires at least two of them.
* `rst_primary_periph_clk_no` is unconnected in `tb_top` and is not claimed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the two edge logs, sample the entry state with every reset observable released.
. Assert `rst_cool_ni` one third into a `clk_smc_i` period; timestamp the reference output's fall, inspect the SMC output at that instant, and check the instant against the `clk_ref_i` edge log. Match the bounded asserted handshake and hold the pin low past the de-glitch window.
. Release `rst_cool_ni` one third into a `clk_ref_i` period; timestamp each output's rise against its own clock's edge log. Match the bounded released handshake.
. Compute the release anchor and the per-domain edge counts, and assert the period relation and the stage minimums.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-RESET-SYNC-ASYNC-ASSERT` |`rst_primary_ref_clk_no` and `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` were both asserted at the same simulation instant, and that instant is a recorded `clk_ref_i` rising edge, so the SMC-domain output asserted without waiting for its own clock
|`CHK-RESET-SYNC-SYNC-DEASSERT` |Each output rose exactly at a recorded rising edge of its own target clock, and the reference output's rise is a whole number of `clk_ref_i` periods after the release anchor
|`CHK-RESET-SYNC-MULTI-STAGE` |At least two `clk_ref_i` edges and at least two `clk_smc_i` edges separate the release anchor from the corresponding output's rise. The measured counts are reported; the specification pins no exact stage count
|`CHK-RESET-SYNC-REF-ANCHOR` |Both the assertion instant and the release anchor are `clk_ref_i` rising edges, which is what makes the reference domain the synchronization anchor for these crossings
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |One line per bounded reset leg the run took, naming the bound and the `clk_ref_i` cycle at which the expected levels matched. The compare is the scoreboard's -- an expired bound raises there -- so these lines are the record of the bounded legs, not a separate compare
|`CHK-RESET-SYNC-EDGE` |Ledger line in the leaf: the assert instant, both release timestamps and both stage counts. Logged after the leaf requires at least two booked reset `WAIT_STATE` checks and requires the sequence to have timestamped every edge it claims
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the two outputs assert in the same `clk_ref_i`-anchored instant, each releases on a rising edge of its own clock, the reference release is a whole number of reference periods after the anchor, both stage counts are at least two, and the scoreboard books at least two reset `WAIT_STATE` checks. `rst_primary_periph_clk_no` is not part of the claim. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


==== CLOCK

[[sec-063-smc_dma_cg_activity_test]]
===== 63 `smc_dma_cg_activity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dma_cg_activity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI), `SmcJtagAxiAgent` (JTAG-AXI bench agent over the `tb_top` JTAG-AXI bridge)

*Objective*: LIVE DMA activity/enable clock-gating check.

*Test Coverage*:

* DV-CARD: SMC_DMA_CG_ACTIVITY_TEST ANCHOR: smc_dma_cg_activity_test DV-CARD: SMC_CG_P2_001 ANCHOR: smc_dma_cg_activity_test The P2 card shares this anchor: the P1 steps and checkers emit their evidence tokens verbatim, and `_p2_extension`, called once at the end of `body()`, adds the full-range hysteresis sweep {0,1,32,63,64} and the activity-reassert race.
* CG_HYSTERESIS_W==6 (hw/sys/smc/doc/dma.adoc) -- gap=64 exercises the field's own truncation (64 & 0x3F == 0), not a TB special case, and its expectation is the truncated value 0, not a `<= 63` bound that no RTL can violate.
* Sweep gaps {0, 1, 32, 63, 64} plus seed extras are all driven and all exactly asserted, but only {32, 63, 64} are BOOKED as covered cells: the 0..8 band is booked UNPROVEN and must not be counted as covered by any closure report -- see `P2_LOW_BAND_EXCLUSION_REASON` in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DMA-GATE-OFF` |After the DMA goes idle the gated clock's last toggle lies inside the hysteresis window and zero toggles follow across the idle observation window; a late or leaking gate fails
|`CHK-DMA-GATING-DISABLED` |With DMA clock gating disabled the clock toggles on every cycle of the observation window
|`CHK-DMA-HYST-RACE` |Named compare in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-DMA-HYST-SWEEP` |Named compare in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-DMA-WAKEUP-BACKEND` |Named compare in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-DMA-WAKEUP-FRONTEND` |A new DMA start wakes the gated clock within one cycle of busy and the clock free-runs for the whole wake-up window; a late resume or a missing edge fails
|`CHK-NONVAC` |Non-vacuity count in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`
|`CHK-NONVAC-P2` |Named compare in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |Named compare in `smc_dma_cg_activity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DMA-GATE-OFF`, `CHK-DMA-GATING-DISABLED`, `CHK-DMA-HYST-RACE`, `CHK-DMA-HYST-SWEEP`, `CHK-DMA-WAKEUP-BACKEND`, `CHK-DMA-WAKEUP-FRONTEND` (and 3 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `clock`.
'''''

[[sec-064-smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test]]
===== 64 `smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS I2C clock-gate sanity scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* Sequence for smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test.
* Dispatches one SmcI2cItem SAMPLE.
* The agent driver reads `tb_i2c_cg_en` / `tb_i2c_debug_lo` and broadcasts the result; the scoreboard exact-compares the post-reset clock-gate default `tb_i2c_cg_en == 0`.
* That compare is a negative check, and the scoreboard *silently* downgrades it to `OBSERVED-ONLY (NOT checked evidence)` when the run holds no liveness credit for `tb_i2c_cg_en` -- it does not fail.
* The sequence-side gate below therefore consumes the retained sample into the property that makes the run evidence at all: the probe carries a same-run credit (so a stuck-at-0 / mis-bound net is excluded) **and** the sample read the gated default.
* The credit is fed by the passive ledger in `env/smc_probe_liveness.py` from a DUT observation of the probe at 1, produced here by the test's `probe_positive_controls = ("i2c_cg_en",)` ([NEGATIVE-NEEDS-POSITIVE-CONTROL]).
* `tb_i2c_debug_lo` stays observed-only: it has no SPEC/RDL-sourced reset value in this environment, so this sequence states no expectation for it.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-CG-SANITY-GATED` |Named compare in `smc_i2c_cg_sanity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-I2C-CG-SANITY-GATED` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.
'''''

|`CHK-ZEROER-AXICLK-NOGLITCH` |Named compare in `smc_zeroer_axiclk_cg_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-NONVAC`, `CHK-NONVAC-P2`, `CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS`, `CHK-ZAXI-BUSY-ENABLE`, `CHK-ZAXI-DISABLE-CG`, `CHK-ZAXI-GATE-OFF-IDLE` (and 3 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `clock`.

[[sec-182-smc_clock_domain_connectivity_test]]
===== 182 `smc_clock_domain_connectivity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_clock_domain_connectivity_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver for the UART CSRs; `tb_uart0_tx_from_dut` timestamped at simulation-time resolution

*Objective*: The UART 16550 is clocked from `clk_periph_i`: an all-zero frame holds TX low for nine bit times of 16 x (DIVISOR + 1) peripheral-clock periods, within two peripheral clocks.

*Test Coverage*:

* Before the measurement the sequence requires the run's `clk_periph_i` and `clk_smc_i` periods to differ, and requires the same frame clocked from `clk_smc_i` to fall outside the tolerance. Without that, the compare could not tell the two domains apart on this seed.
* TX is required idle-high after the divisor programming and before `THR` is written; the start of the frame is bounded at three bit times of `THR`, and the low pulse is bounded at twice the expected width. Both bounds raise rather than being absorbed.
* The expected pulse is computed from the programmed divisor and the run's configured peripheral-clock period, not read back from the DUT.
* Only the UART cell of the peripheral-clock scenario is closed. The AVSBus, I2C and I3C cells need their bus protocols in flight, the PLL wrapper is a behavioural model here with no reference-clock consumer to observe, and `rst_telemetry_ni` is tied to the cold reset in `tb_top`.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_clock_domain_connectivity_test_seq` on the SEP_IN AXI sequencer: ungate the UART clock, enable UART0, program the divisor through `LCR.DLAB`, confirm TX idle-high, write an all-zero byte to `THR`, timestamp the falling and rising edge of the frame's low pulse, compare it with nine peripheral-clock bit times, and restore the clock-gate register. The leaf then books a protocol-VIP record against a stimulus floor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-ON-CLK-PERIPH` |The measured low pulse equals nine bit times of the run's `clk_periph_i` period within two peripheral clocks. Logged only after that compare holds; the line also prints what the same frame would have measured on `clk_smc_i`, which the sequence proved lies outside the tolerance
|`CHK-CLOCK-DOMAIN-NOT-CLOSED` |A recorded gap, not a compare. Names the cells of this scenario the bench cannot drive -- avsbus/i2c/i3c-on-clk-periph, clk-ref-reaches-pll-wrapper, telemetry-reset-asserted/released -- each with its reason. Logged unconditionally
|`CHK-CLOCK-DOMAIN-CONNECTIVITY` |Ledger line in the leaf: the measured pulse, the expected pulse and the tolerance, logged after the leaf requires the sequence to have produced a measurement at all. The compare itself is the sequence's; this line carries none of its own
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the UART0 all-zero frame holds TX low for nine `clk_periph_i` bit times within two peripheral clocks, having been proven distinguishable from the `clk_smc_i` alternative on this seed. The other cells of the peripheral-clock scenario are recorded as not closed and are not part of the claim. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


==== CPU

[[sec-069-smc_cluster_beu_test]]
===== 69 `smc_cluster_beu_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cluster_beu_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Locks the 0xC801_0000 -> 0xC001_0000 local-fabric address fold.

*Test Coverage*:

* **Read aliasing.** Each documented BEU address and the `0xC001_xxxx` address `local_fabric_masked_addr` maps it to are read in the same run and must return the SAME word. Only the mapped read carries an `expected=`, and only where the register behind it has a generated RDL reset; the BEU-address read is pinned by nothing except the pair compare, so that compare is the sole check on it and fails if the fold is repaired.
* **Write co-residency.** A distinguishing hysteresis value is written to `0xC801_0018` and read back at `0xC001_0018`. One register cannot be two registers, so this is positive evidence of a single physical location behind both addresses -- a property no read-side `expected=` restates, and one that temporal isolation cannot manufacture, because the write and the readback address different words of the address space.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cluster_beu_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-BEU-WINDOW-ALIASED` |Named compare in `smc_cluster_beu_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-BEU-WINDOW-ALIASED` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-070-smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test]]
===== 70 `smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Run CPU-control address-map CSR depth checks.

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC_BASE_CONFIG (CPU address-map + hang-detector) reset-value precheck.
* The block at `smc_addr("SMC_TOP_SMC_BASE_CONFIG_BASE_ADDR")` is SMC_BASE_CONFIG (`hw/sys/smc/regs/blocks/smc_base_config/smc_base_config.rdl`), reached over real SEP_IN AXI.
* Every row of :data:`CPU_MAP_READS` addresses a **real register that exists in the generated map**, by its own generated `SMC_TOP_SMC_BASE_CONFIG_*_BASE_ADDR` symbol, and expects that register's **generated RDL reset constant**, imported by symbol from `hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/c/blocks/smc_base_config.h` -- never a hand literal and never an address computed as `BASE_ADDR + <offset>`.
* So every read verifies decode *and* spec-defined reset content.
* No row addresses `0xC001_0050`.
* It is not "reserved/open-bus space within the block window": `SMC_TOP_SMC_BASE_CONFIG_BASE_ADDR` is `0xC0010000` and `SMC_TOP_SMC_BASE_CONFIG_SIZE` is `0x0000004C` (`hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/c/smc_addr.h:108-109`), so the window ends at `0xC001004B` and `0xC0010050` is outside it.
* Undecoded space reads back `0x00000000`, which is exactly what the row expected, so it could not fail on any RTL while logging a register-shaped name that names no register (`[ADDRESS-FROM-AUTHORITATIVE-MAP]`).
* The sweep reads `REGION_SIZE` instead: a real register of the same block with a non-zero generated reset, so the row fails if the fabric ever stops decoding it.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-BFM-OBSERVABILITY` |Named compare in `smc_cpu_vip_utils.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CPU-CTRL-MAP-DEPTH` |Named compare in `smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-CPU-CTRL-MAP-LIVE` |Named compare in `smc_cpu_ctrl_map_depth_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-CPU-BFM-OBSERVABILITY`, `CHK-CPU-CTRL-MAP-DEPTH`, `CHK-CPU-CTRL-MAP-LIVE` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-075-smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test]]
===== 75 `smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the codeword corruption is a backdoor write
through the `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_*` pins of the bound `smc_cpu_mem_dv` collateral

*Objective*: Two flipped codeword bits: the CPU raises cluster_ded_o.

*Test Coverage*:

* U7-1 / P2-7 scratch ECC codeword corrupted in the macro array: two flipped bits raise DED.

*Test Procedure*:

The DUT offers no frontdoor way to corrupt a stored codeword, so the sequence
XORs two bits into scratch bank0 entry 0 through `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_en` /
`tb_cpu_ecc_poke_entry` / `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_mask` before the CPU boot release;
that pulse is the only write this leaf makes into DUT state. The reaction is
observed frontdoor: `smc_cluster_ded_o` on the wrapper (held sticky in
`tb_cluster_ded_seen`) must assert after the reset-vector fetch, and the bound
module's scratch read counter must have advanced, so the DED came from a read
of the poked word and not from a bench that never fetched.

Drive `smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ECC-CODEWORD-DED` |Named compare in `smc_ecc_codeword_corrupt_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-ECC-CODEWORD-DED` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-076-smc_ecc_codeword_sec_test]]

through the `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_*` pins of the bound `smc_cpu_mem_dv` collateral

*Objective*: One flipped codeword bit: the CPU reads the bank and DED stays low.

*Test Coverage*:

* U7-1 / P2-7 scratch ECC codeword corrupted in the macro array: one flipped bit is benign.

*Test Procedure*:

The DUT offers no frontdoor way to corrupt a stored codeword, so the sequence
XORs one bit into scratch bank0 entry 0 through `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_en` /
`tb_cpu_ecc_poke_entry` / `tb_cpu_ecc_poke_mask` before the CPU boot release;
that pulse is the only write this leaf makes into DUT state. The reaction is
observed frontdoor: the bound module's scratch read counter must advance (the
CPU fetched the poked word) and `smc_cluster_ded_o` on the wrapper, held sticky
in `tb_cluster_ded_seen`, must stay 0 for 20 000 further cycles. The paired
`smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test` is the positive control that the same tap does
assert on a two-bit fault.

Drive `smc_ecc_codeword_sec_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

|===

'''''

The count itself is reported, not compared. The programmer's guide states this harness reads 4
|`CHK-DIAG-NDMRESET-CLUSTER-COUNT-RO` |Named compare in `smc_ecc_dfd_dbs_sanity_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-BANK-AXIL-ACTIVE` |Positive control in `smc_efuse_vip_utils.py`, reached through `prove_axil_any_master_activity`: `tb_axil_efuse_bank_active` samples 1 while a strict SEP_IN read of the eFuse-shim window (expected its generated reset) is in flight; the run fails if the probe never rises
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DIAG-AXIL-ACTIVE`, `CHK-DIAG-AXIL-IDLE`, `CHK-DIAG-CSR-COUNT`, `CHK-DIAG-CSR-DFX_DEBUG_BUS_MUX`, `CHK-DIAG-CSR-DFX_DEBUG_CTRL`, `CHK-DIAG-CSR-NDMRESET_PROCESS` (and 3 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-078-smc_ecc_fault_inject_test]]
===== 78 `smc_ecc_fault_inject_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_ecc_fault_inject_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `tb_cpu_ecc_inject_sbe` / `tb_cpu_ecc_inject_dbe`
are testbench pins into the bound `smc_cpu_mem_dv` collateral, not product ports

*Objective*: The scratch bank0 ECC inject hook is reached during FW boot and is gated by its enable pins.

*Test Coverage*:

* U7-1 / P2-7: ECC SBE/DBE inject hooks on scratch bank0.
* DEFENDS: with inject armed, a real scratch bank0 read advances tb_cpu_ecc_inject_fire_count via DUT cpu_scratch0_inject_fire; clearing inject holds that counter while further scratch reads are still arriving. The claim is the contrast between those two halves of the run -- the hold on its own is true for every DUT state, because both inject pins are TB inputs and both are 0.
* DOES NOT DEFEND: Rocket ECC syndrome CSR / precise RAS recovery FW policy. Nor any DUT SECDED behaviour: the hook counts qualifying reads and corrupts no data -- there is no force on the macro response in this bench.

*Test Procedure*:

The inject pins arm a hook in the bound DV memory collateral that counts
qualifying bank0 reads; the hook writes nothing into DUT state and forces no
DUT signal. The claim is scored on the hook's own fire counter against the
CPU's real scratch reads, so no DUT SECDED behaviour is claimed here -- the
stored-codeword vehicles `smc_ecc_codeword_sec_test` and
`smc_ecc_codeword_ded_test` carry that.

Drive `smc_ecc_fault_inject_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ECC-INJECT` |Emitted only after three asserted compares on `tb_cpu_ecc_inject_fire_count`: it advanced while SBE inject was armed, held while both inject pins were clear across further scratch reads, and advanced again while DBE inject was armed (`smc_ecc_fault_inject_test_seq.py`); any of the three failing fails the run before the token
|`CHK-ECC-INJECT-NO-DUT-SECDED` |Carries no compare of its own: a scope statement that this leaf proves the inject hook and its enable gating, not the DUT's SECDED detector, which `smc_ecc_codeword_corrupt_test` covers
|`CHK-ECC-INJECT-RECOVERY` |Reports both halves of the contrast: the armed advance and the cleared hold, with the scratch-read counts they were taken over
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-ECC-INJECT`, `CHK-ECC-INJECT-NO-DUT-SECDED`, `CHK-ECC-INJECT-RECOVERY` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-079-smc_mutex_semaphore_test]]
===== 79 `smc_mutex_semaphore_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_mutex_semaphore_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: CPU_CTRL MUTEX/SEMA over SEP_IN AXI (expectations from cpu_ctrl.rdl).

*Test Coverage*:

* `reg MUTEX` (rdl:270-281), `field ... mutex[0:0] = 0x1`, desc: "HW mutex. Reads will attempt to acquire mutex, 1 on success. If the mutex is already acquired, the read will return 0. To release the mutex, write any value to the register." `MUTEX[4] @ 0x240` (rdl:378) -- four *independent* locks.
* `reg SEMA` (rdl:283-293), `field ... sema[15:0] = 0x0`, desc: "16-bit semaphore value to inc/dec. Writing to this register will inc/dec the semaphore value. The written value is treated as a signed number using 2s compliment." `SEMA[4] @ 0x260` (rdl:379).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq`. The scenario has two implementations that share this
claim: the cocotb `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM
`smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.svh` (`--framework uvm`). Each checker below is a named compare
the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-MUTEX-BASIC` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-HELD` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-NEIGHBOUR-FREE` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-NEIGHBOUR-HOLD` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-REL` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-TAKE` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-SEMA-ACCUMULATE` |Named compare in `smc_mutex_semaphore_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-MUTEX-DENY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the second read of a held `MUTEX[0]` returns 0. Only meaningful because the first read expected the available encoding and got it -- a mutex stuck at 0 fails the take leg, one stuck at 1 fails this one
|`CHK-MUTEX-INDEP` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): `MUTEX[1]` still reports available while `MUTEX[0]` is held, so "acquired" is a property of one lock rather than of the block
|`CHK-MUTEX-RELEASE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): after a write, `MUTEX[0]` reports available again. The RDL permits *any* value to release; both implementations write zero, so the property is proved for that value and the wider claim is not exercised
|`CHK-SEMA-ACCUM` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): `SEMA[0]` accumulates a signed 16-bit increment; +N then -N returns it to its baseline, and the baseline expectation of zero is also the check that the previous pass restored it
|`CHK-CSR-RESP` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): every SEP_IN CSR access completes with an OKAY response
|`CHK-FUSE-SENSE-DONE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): warm-reset domain release after fuse sense completes
|`CHK-NONVAC` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): reconciles the accesses the body issued against its own loop bounds -- twelve per pass, seven reads and five writes. A control-flow invariant, not a DUT-failable compare: it catches a truncated pass, and `CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` is the observed-side counterpart
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the `mutex_sema` feature's comparison count, taken from the always-on scoreboard fed by the PASSIVE monitor, is at or above the floor the completed passes imply (seven predicted reads per pass)
|`CHK-SB-MUTEX-SEMA` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): always-on scoreboard, feature `mutex_sema`: `smc_mutex_sema_ref_model` models acquire-on-read and the signed accumulator, computing each expected value BEFORE the state advances, and compares under the RDL field mask
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-MUTEX-BASIC`, `CHK-MUTEX-HELD`, `CHK-MUTEX-NEIGHBOUR-FREE`, `CHK-MUTEX-NEIGHBOUR-HOLD`, `CHK-MUTEX-REL`, `CHK-MUTEX-TAKE` (and 1 more) hold after their compares.

Both registers are declared regwidth 64 with their live field inside the low 32 bits, so only
the RDL field bits are compared -- the bits above the field hold no field and are masked out
rather than given an invented expectation.

A mutex read acquires, so the SV-UVM view -- 16 scenario passes in one simulation -- hands the
block back as it found it: `MUTEX[0]` is released again after the read that proves the release,
`MUTEX[1]` is released, and `SEMA[0]` is returned to its baseline. Under `--framework uvm` -- a
framework no run in this package executes, so the SV-UVM rows above are not closed by any run
here -- the SV-UVM checkers above hold instead, and `+SMC_MUTEX_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run.
Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''


[[sec-081-smc_reference_counter_test]]
===== 81 `smc_reference_counter_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_reference_counter_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: 64-bit refclk counter rate check; not the OCTS timer.

*Test Coverage*:

* CPU_CTRL REFERENCE_COUNTER is a refclk CDC counter, not the OCTS timer.
* EXPECT-SOURCE (SPEC, not RTL): `doc/trm/src/architecture.adoc:256-265` -- the `REFERENCE_COUNTER` CSR is a free-running 64-bit counter "clocked by the always-on reference clock (`clk_ref_i`)", advancing "continuously from reset, independent of the system clock (`clk_smu_i`) frequency or PLL state", synchronised into the CPU clock domain for software reads.
* One count per `clk_ref_i` rising edge is therefore a SPEC-derived rate, not a transcription of `smc_cpu_ctrl_wrap.sv` / `prim_refclk_count_w_cdc` ([INDEPENDENT-EXPECTED-MODEL]).
* The check below turns that rate into a two-sided bound on the observed delta, measured over the very interval the two CSR samples bracket -- so a counter clocked by `clk_smc_i` (faster), by `clk_periph_i`, or at half rate fails, which a bare `c1 > c0` floor would pass.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_reference_counter_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-REF-COUNT` |Named compare in `smc_reference_counter_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-REF-COUNT-BASIC` |Named compare in `smc_reference_counter_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-REF-COUNT`, `CHK-REF-COUNT-BASIC` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-082-smc_spm_mem_boundary_test]]
===== 82 `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: SPM first/next/last 64-bit word write/readback via SEP_IN AXI.

*Test Coverage*:

* SPM window edges via SEP_IN AXI.
* Real-stall / multi-hart traffic is not claimed.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq`. The scenario has two implementations that share this
claim: the cocotb `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM
`smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.svh` (`--framework uvm`). Both write ALL the edge patterns
before reading any of them back: writing and reading one address at a time cannot see aliasing,
because each write would be followed immediately by its own read and every pair would still
match. With the writes batched, a decode that collapsed the edges onto one location returns the
last pattern written and the first readback fails. Each checker below is a named compare the
DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SPM-MEM-BASIC` |Named compare in `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_HI` |Named compare in `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_LO` |Named compare in `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_LO_NEXT` |Named compare in `smc_spm_mem_boundary_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-SPM-MEM-EDGE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): each window edge reads back the 64-bit word written to it while the other two edge patterns are still resident
|`CHK-SPM-CO-RESIDENT` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): each edge's readback is required NOT to equal any other edge's pattern -- the shape a collapsed decode would produce. Addresses and patterns are asserted pairwise distinct before the legs run, since identical patterns would make aliasing indistinguishable from correctness
|`CHK-MEM-RESP` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): every SEP_IN full-width access completes with an OKAY response
|`CHK-FUSE-SENSE-DONE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): warm-reset domain release after fuse sense completes
|`CHK-NONVAC` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): reconciles the accesses the body issued against its own loop bounds -- a control-flow invariant that catches a truncated pass, not a DUT-failable compare. `CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` is the observed-side counterpart
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): the `spm_mem` feature's comparison count, taken from the always-on scoreboard fed by the PASSIVE monitor, is at or above the floor the completed passes imply (one predicted readback per window edge per pass)
|`CHK-SB-SPM-MEM` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`; no run in this package executes that framework, so this row is not closed by any run here): always-on scoreboard, feature `spm_mem`: `smc_spm_mem_ref_model` shadows the window per 64-bit word address, so an aliased decode disagrees with the model that keeps the edges apart
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-SPM-MEM-BASIC`, `CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_HI`, `CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_LO`, `CHK-SPM-MEM-SPM_LO_NEXT` hold after their compares.

Accesses are full-width 64-bit single beats (AxSIZE = 3, every byte lane strobed): a 32-bit
CSR-shaped access would leave half of each word untouched and could not discriminate the
addresses. The SV-UVM view draws a fresh pattern set per pass from the scenario seed, so its 16
passes exercise different data and each pass also re-proves that the previous pass's words were
replaced rather than merely re-read. Under `--framework uvm` -- a framework no run in this package executes, so the SV-UVM rows above are not closed by any run here -- the governing checker set is the SV-UVM one in the table above -- the cocotb ids named at the head of this Pass Criteria do not run there -- and it holds
instead, and `+SMC_SPM_MEM_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-080-smc_occp_sanity_secure_error_test]]
===== 80 `smc_occp_sanity_secure_error_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_occp_sanity_secure_error_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Secure program-fail completes and reports done; recovery burn programs bit0; signature positive gate.

*Test Coverage*:

* OTP PROGRAM failure injection (secure programming error), via `+smc_efuse_prog_fail_count`.
* DEFENDS: on the injected-failure burn the eFuse controller still reaches PROGRAM_DONE (bounded poll, expiry fails), and the subsequent recovery burn really does sticky-OR bit0 of word0 through the unmodified data path. The signature positive gate reads CHIP_CONFIG / EFUSE_MAP word0 against their expected values.
* DOES NOT DEFEND, model-backed: "the failing burn left the OTP word unchanged" is *not DUT-earned*. `hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/efuse_bank_model.sv:120` drives the macro as `.s_apb_pwdata(prog_fail_act ? 32'h0 : apb_req_i.pwdata)`, so on the failing write the DV model substitutes 0 for whatever the controller put on the bus and the word cannot change whatever the controller does. The compare stands as a consistency check on the injection hook only. Same tap as testcase 95, which already carries this disclaimer.
* GAP: the fail path is DUT-observable only if the model rejects the burn at the APB handshake (`pslverr`, macro write suppressed) instead of rewriting the data; the model is shared between the SEP and SMC benches.
* No mismatched signature is injected -- no write, plusarg, image variant or force on the proof path produces one, and the only `+smc_efuse_hex` image's word 0 *is* the golden. The positive gate is what runs.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_occp_sanity_secure_error_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCCP-SECURE-ERROR-RECOVERY` |EFUSE_MAP word0 matches the preload marker and CHIP_CONFIG_VERSION_LO its constant; the injected program failure reaches PROGRAM_DONE within the poll bound with word0 unchanged; the recovery burn reaches PROGRAM_DONE and sticky-ORs bit0 of word0. Fails on any mismatch, a poll that expires, or a non-OKAY SEP_IN response
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-OCCP-SECURE-ERROR-RECOVERY` holds after its compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

==== JTAG

[[sec-090-smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test]]
===== 90 `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: SPI pad BFM, ocah_axi_vip (AXI), ocah_axi_vip (AXI-Lite), ocah_jtag_vip

*Objective*: Drive lc_state_i + JTAG eFuse accesses; assert the block/allow matrix.

*Test Coverage*:

* `tb_lc_state` drives `lc_state_i` = {diff_n, diff_p} directly, and
* the `ej_axi` AXI-Lite master drives `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_i`.
* The expected matrix is taken from the specification, not the RTL: `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Lifecycle State (LC_STATE) Effects", cited below by line.
* lifecycle encodings, :265-282 -- `TEST_DEV 0x0 | PROD 0x1 | RMA_SOP 0x2, 0x3 | RMA_CHIPLET 0x6-0x7 | PROD_END 0x8 | INVALID all others`;
* the restricted set, :286 -- "When lifecycle state is PROD (0x1) or RMA_SOP (0x2, 0x3), JTAG access to eFuse registers is restricted";
* the access table, :289-295 -- JTAG Write *Blocked* / JTAG Read *Blocked* except Chiplet ID / JTAG Read Chiplet ID Allowed, in both columns;
* the Chiplet ID exception, :301-305 -- "Even in PROD or RMA_SOP states, JTAG read access to the chiplet ID and package ID registers is permitted";
* the blocked response, :297-299 -- "the error slave returns an error response with data value `0xbadcab1e`".

The matrix below is the specification's outcome per raw `lc_state_i`
encoding; the encodings and state names are the specification's
(`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc:265-282`). The checkers drive one
encoding per state -- `0x0`, `0x1`, `0x2`, `0x6`, `0x8` -- plus the
integrity-error case; `0x3` and `0x7`, the specification's second encodings
of RMA_SOP and RMA_CHIPLET, are not driven. The integrity-error row is not a
specification table row: it is the `INVALID ... (error fallback)` class
(`:281`) applied fail-closed, with no identity exception.
`smc_efuse_wrapper` gates JTAG-to-eFuse access on `lc_state_i`; a blocked
transaction returns DECERR with data `0xBADCAB1E`. `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_negative_test`
covers the CSR-observable subset (`CHIP_CONFIG_LC_STATE` plus the always-allowed
JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY reads).

[cols="2,2,2,3",options="header",]
|===
|SMC LC state (raw) |JTAG eFuse write |JTAG eFuse read (non-ID) |JTAG read JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY
|TEST_DEV `0x0` |allow |allow |allow
|PROD `0x1` |BLOCK (DECERR/`0xBADCAB1E`) |BLOCK |allow
|RMA_SOP `0x2`,`0x3` |BLOCK |BLOCK |allow
|RMA_CHIPLET `0x6`,`0x7` |allow |allow |allow
|PROD_END `0x8` |allow |allow |allow
|differential-decode integrity error |BLOCK |BLOCK |BLOCK (no ID exception)
|===

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-PROD` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-PROD_END` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-RMA_CHIPLET` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-RMA_SOP` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-SIGINT` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-TEST_DEV` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-PROD`, `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-PROD_END`, `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-RMA_CHIPLET`, `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-RMA_SOP`, `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-SIGINT`, `CHK-EFUSE-JTAG-LC-TEST_DEV` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-097-smc_efuse_permission_boundary_test]]
===== 97 `smc_efuse_permission_boundary_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_permission_boundary_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Read the four eFuse-derived chip-config registers over SEP_IN AXI on the CSR side of the eFuse boundary, with the eFuse-bank AXI-Lite positive control and idle leg.

*Test Coverage*:

* DEFENDS: the four eFuse-derived chip-config registers decode; `VERSION_LO`, `VERSION_HI` and `CHIP_ID` read their generated reset values, `LC_STATE` is decode-only because its value is simulator-divergent in the OSS bench; and the eFuse-bank AXI-Lite probe is observed going 0 -> 1 -> 0 (positive control then idle leg).
* DOES NOT DEFEND: eFuse permission semantics. This testcase runs the same `smc_efuse_chip_config_read_test_seq` body as testcase 86 -- it programs no lock, drives no lifecycle transition and attempts no denied access, because the SMC CSR boundary exposes none of that. Lifecycle-gated access control is proven by `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test` and locked-shadow access by `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test`; the raw OTP window has no responder on `smc_wrapper` and is recorded under Known Limitations.
* PROXY: the protocol-VIP record this leaf files carries `proxy=True` and names what it stands in for -- the raw OTP permission paths -- and what it did not do (no lock programmed, no lifecycle transition, no denied access, no raw OTP window touched). The record is not feature credit for that path.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_chip_config_read_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[[sec-098-smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test]]
===== 98 `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: timeout_enable|0 aborts; default timeout recovers OTP bit0.

*Test Coverage*:

* PROGRAM/READ timeout CSRs.
* timeout_enable|0 fires in ST_WAIT_RESP on this shim.
* **The claim is split, and only one half is a DUT-path claim.** *DUT path (reportable).* `EFUSE_PROGRAM_REQ_TIMEOUT` / `EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` with `timeout_enable=1, cycles=0` must abort the outstanding request and raise `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL.PROGRAM_STATUS` / leave `EFUSE_READ_INTERFACE_READ_DATA` empty; re-programming the RDL-default cycle count must let the same operation complete.
* That FSM is real `efuse_interface_controller` RTL and every address, field mask and reset used here is imported by generated symbol from `efuse_interface_ctrl.h`.
* *Model-backed (NOT silicon-path coverage).* `tb_efuse_programmed_word0` is a TB tap on `u_dut.u_smc_ip_integration.u_efuse_bank_model.u_efuse_bank_reg` (`tb/tb_top.sv:1274-1277`).
* SPEC declares that block a stand-in: "The eFuse bank model (`efuse_bank_model.sv`) is a reference, simulation-only stand-in for the real foundry OTP macro ...
* In a production integration it is replaced by the actual foundry macro driven by the SHIM" (`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc:163-170`), and its set-once semantics are `onwrite = woset` in the DV RDL `hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/regs/efuse_bank.rdl:14`.
* The `OTP=...` observations below therefore show that the controller's command did or did not reach the bank model -- they are **not** proof that a fuse burns in silicon ([BEHAVIORAL-STUB-DECLARED], §2 real-path/no-HW-equivalent).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-READ-STATUS-SET-ON-NO-ENABLE` |Positive control for the `READ_STATUS == 0` compares: with `STATUS.EFUSE_REQ_ERROR` read clear first, the recovery read's command word minus `READ_ENABLE` sets `READ_STATUS` in `EFUSE_READ_CTRL`, the cause `efuse_interface_ctrl.rdl` names for the field; `READ_DATA` is logged, not asserted (`smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-BASIC` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG-REC` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG-SET` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD-REC` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_read_program_timeout_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-READ-STATUS-SET-ON-NO-ENABLE`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-BASIC`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG-REC`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-PROG-SET`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD` (and 1 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-099-smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test]]
===== 99 `smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI) against the eFuse bank model

*Objective*: A read that hits `EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` completes with `EFUSE_READ_CTRL.READ_STATUS` set, after a successful read shows it clear.

*Test Coverage*:

* `hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_interface_ctrl.rdl` defines `EFUSE_READ_CTRL.READ_STATUS` as the read interface's error report (`read_done` says the read completed, `read_status` says how) and `EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` as a bound on the cycles the interface waits for a SHIM response; `hw/ip/efuse/doc/programming.adoc` has software check `read_status` after every completion. A read that hits the bound is a completed read with an error: `read_done=1` and `read_status=1`.
* The arming leg makes the check real: `READ_STATUS` holds until the next completion, so a stale 1 would satisfy the timeout leg on its own. A successful read at the RDL-default cycle count comes first and must show `READ_STATUS` 0; `STATUS.EFUSE_REQ_ERROR` is required clear beforehand because a sticky request error also reports through `READ_STATUS`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Issue a successful read at the RDL default READ_TMO and record READ_CTRL with READ_STATUS clear.
. Require STATUS.EFUSE_REQ_ERROR clear, so a sticky req-err cannot be mistaken for the timeout path.
. Force the read to time out and read READ_CTRL again.
. Require READ_STATUS to be set.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD-STATUS-ARM` |A successful read at the RDL-default timeout completes with `READ_DONE` set and `READ_STATUS` clear, with `STATUS.EFUSE_REQ_ERROR` clear, arming the leg below (`smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD-STATUS-SET` |The read that hits the programmed timeout completes with `READ_DONE` set and `READ_STATUS` set (`smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test_seq.py`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD-STATUS-ARM`, `CHK-EFUSE-TMO-RD-STATUS-SET` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-101-smc_state_fault_inject_test]]
===== 101 `smc_state_fault_inject_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_state_fault_inject_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI) for the CSR path; state corruption is a force
on the state flops through the testbench's `tb_efuse_program_state_inject`,
`tb_efuse_read_state_inject` and `tb_zeroer_state_inject` hooks (each with its
`_en`); the read errors are forces through `tb_efuse_read_error_inject` on the
eFuse interface response status, `efuse_req_err_i` and `secure_tm_blocked_i`;
and the SYS_OUT responder's observability counters (`tb_output_axi_write_count`,
`tb_output_axi_last_addr`, `tb_output_axi_last_wdata`) for the Zeroer's fabric
traffic

*Objective*: Every unused state encoding in the eFuse interface controller and in the Zeroer fails closed, and the block recovers.

*Test Coverage*:

* Directed fault injection into two state machines: the eFuse interface
  controller's program and read FSMs, and the Zeroer FSM.
* eFuse: both unused 2-bit encodings (`0b00` and `0b11`) are injected, on the
  program path and the read path independently.
* The same-cycle claim is the point of the `0b11` case -- the corruption is armed
  concurrently with a legal `GO` write, and the injector asserts the request line
  is suppressed in that same cycle rather than one cycle later.
* Recovery is scored, not just entry: after injection the FSM must return to
  `IDLE` with request low, busy low, done high and error high, and the program
  read-back must read the `0xBADCAB1E` illegal-state sentinel.
* Non-vacuity is a real baseline: a legal program runs first and the sequence
  asserts its read-back does *not* already equal the sentinel, so the
  post-injection compare could have failed.
* Read-error sourcing: a new legal request must clear a prior read error, and an
  injected read error must set the status and zero the read-back.
* Zeroer: all four illegal encodings (`0b011`, `0b101`, `0b110`, `0b111`) must
  enter the absorbing `ERROR` state, hold it for at least two further cycles with
  busy high, `intp` low and both AXI valids low, and only a cold reset escapes it.
* Each of the four "start write is ignored in `ERROR`" legs has its own live
  control, run immediately before its injection: the pass-all inbound/outbound
  output filters are programmed, then the same `CTRL_STATUS` write (`INT_EN`,
  packed from the generated `ZEROER_CTRL_CTRL_STATUS_reg_t` layout), with
  `DEST_ADDR`/`SIZE` programmed to one 8-byte beat at the output fabric, starts
  the Zeroer from `IDLE`: busy is sampled high, the FSM is seen somewhere other
  than `IDLE` during that busy interval and never in `ERROR`, the SYS_OUT
  responder counts exactly one write beat of zero at that address, and the FSM
  returns to `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` busy clear.
* The control is per encoding because the cold reset that ends each encoding is
  also the output filters' reset (`smc_internal_regs.sv`:
  `filter_ctrl_reg.arst_n` is `rst_primary_smc_clk_ni`), and a read-back after
  that reset returns the reset word rather than pass-all. Programming the
  filters once would leave the deny's "the responder write count did not move"
  able to hold because the SYS_OUT write path was shut, not because the Zeroer
  refused the start. With the control immediately before the injection, the
  identical write is measured moving that counter under the same filter
  configuration the deny runs under, and nothing resets the filters between the
  two -- so the deny differs from its control in the FSM state alone.
* Every wait is bounded (10 000 cycles on the FSM and CSR waits, 200 cycles on
  the output-responder observation) and reports the last observed value on
  expiry, so a signal that never moves fails rather than hanging.

*Test Procedure*:

The DUT offers no frontdoor way to reach an unused state encoding, so each
encoding is forced through the `tb_*_state_inject` hook named above and each
read error through `tb_efuse_read_error_inject`. The checks read the state flop
back through the `tb_efuse_program_state` / `tb_efuse_read_state` /
`tb_zeroer_state` taps and the blocks' internal outputs (`tb_efuse_*_busy`,
`tb_efuse_*_done`, `tb_efuse_*_error`, `tb_efuse_*_readback`, `tb_zeroer_*`),
not the control registers; that is why the leaf is held out of `all` and
recorded under Known Limitations.

[arabic]
. Wait for fuse-sense-done.
. Run a legal program command, confirm completion, and record a read-back that is
  not the illegal-state sentinel.
. Inject eFuse program state `0b00` with no request outstanding; confirm the
  request stays low in the corrupted cycle, recovery reaches `IDLE` with done and
  error set, `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` reads done with status set and busy clear, and
  the read-back is the sentinel. Run a legal program and confirm it completes
  without error and its read-back is no longer the sentinel.
. Arm the `0b11` injection concurrently with a legal program `GO`, confirm the
  request is suppressed in the same cycle, then confirm the same recovery and the
  same legal program afterwards.
. Repeat both encodings on the read path, each followed by a legal read that
  completes without error; inject a read error and confirm the status sets and
  the read-back zeroes, then clear it through `REQ_ERROR_CLEAR`.
. For each of the four illegal Zeroer encodings, run its start control first:
  program the inbound/outbound output-fabric filters to pass all, confirm the
  Zeroer is in `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` not reporting busy, program `DEST_ADDR`
  to the output-fabric address and `SIZE` to 8, then write `CTRL_STATUS` with
  `INT_EN` while sampling busy every cycle; confirm busy rose, the FSM was seen
  somewhere other than `IDLE` during the busy interval and never in `ERROR`, the
  SYS_OUT responder saw exactly one write of zero at that address, and the FSM is
  back in `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` busy clear.
. Then, for the same encoding: start from `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` not reporting
  busy, re-arm the same `DEST_ADDR`/`SIZE`, inject, confirm the absorbing `ERROR`
  state holds with `CTRL_STATUS` reporting busy, write `CTRL_STATUS` as the
  control just did and confirm the start is ignored for 200 cycles with the
  responder write count unchanged, then assert cold reset and confirm the escape
  to `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` clear. The cold reset also clears the filters, so
  the next encoding's control re-programs them.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ZEROER-START-CONTROL` |Emitted once per illegal Zeroer encoding, immediately before that encoding's injection: with the pass-all output filters programmed and the Zeroer in `IDLE` with `DEST_ADDR`/`SIZE` set, the `CTRL_STATUS` start write raises busy with the FSM seen outside `IDLE` during the busy interval and never in `ERROR`, the SYS_OUT responder counts exactly one write beat of zero at `DEST_ADDR`, and the FSM returns to `IDLE` with `CTRL_STATUS` busy clear -- so the SYS_OUT write path is measured open under the filter configuration the deny that follows runs under
|`CHK-STATE-FAULT` |eFuse `00`/`11` fail closed on both the program and the read path, with done and status set in `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` / `EFUSE_READ_CTRL`, and a legal operation completes without error afterwards; a new legal request clears a prior read error and an injected read error sets the status; Zeroer `011`/`101`/`110`/`111` each enter the absorbing error state from `IDLE`, report busy in `CTRL_STATUS`, ignore the `CTRL_STATUS` start write that their own control had just run from `IDLE` over a path shown open (state, busy, `intp`, AXI valids and the responder write count all unchanged), and only a cold reset escapes it
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-ZEROER-START-CONTROL` holds for all four illegal Zeroer encodings and `CHK-STATE-FAULT` holds, both after their compares; each deny's unchanged responder write count is preceded by that encoding's own control moving the same counter; and every bounded wait in the sequence completed rather than expiring. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-093-smc_efuse_map_read_test]]
===== 93 `smc_efuse_map_read_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_map_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: I3C VIP, SPI pad BFM, ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: SMC_EFUSE_MAP 1 KiB window direct read.

*Test Coverage*:

* The sweep reads LOCKS lo/hi, `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY`, `SMC_CONFIG`, `OCCP_TRANSPORT_TIMEOUT`, all nine `I2C_I3C_ID` entries, and SPARE[0:1] -- every region that has both a generated base address and a generated `*_READ_LOCK` bit, plus LOCKS itself.
* readable words -- `efuse_preload_word_at(addr)` parses `hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/smc_efuse_default.hex` at run time and indexes it by `(addr - SMC_TOP_SMC_EFUSE_MAP_BASE_ADDR)/4`, the same word ordering `efuse_bank_model.sv:140-160` uses. Regenerating the asset moves the expectation with it instead of leaving a stale literal asserting a false identity.
* read-locked words -- `efuse_map_read_locked()` reads the `*_READ_LOCK` bit out of the *preloaded* `LOCKS` word using the generated bit position from `regs/gen/c/blocks/smc_efuse_map.h`; SPEC then fixes the data a blocked read returns: "When a request is blocked, the error slave returns an error response with data value `0xbadcab1e`" (`architecture.adoc:297-299`), and `lock[0] = 1` is "read-locked" (`architecture.adoc:198`).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_map_read_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-MAP-READ` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_map_read_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-MAP-READ` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-088-smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test]]
===== 88 `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Sense completes under ext_boot hold; delayed fuse_reset_n waits release.

*Test Coverage*:

* `LOCKS` -- read from `hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/smc_efuse_default.hex` at run time via `efuse_preload_word_at` rather than a hand-transcribed literal, which cannot detect the asset and the model disagreeing. That asset is what `efuse_bank_model.sv:140-160` `$readmemh`s under the bench-wide `+smc_efuse_hex` (`smc_sim_cfg.toml:386` and `:392`), so this leg's proof class is **transport** (the map returns the sensed word), not fuse programming ([NO-UNJUSTIFIED-PRELOAD]).
* `EFUSE_PROGRAM_INTERFACE_READ_DATA` -- the generated RDL reset `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL__EFUSE_PROGRAM_INTERFACE_READ_DATA__DOUT_reset` (`hw/ip/efuse/regs/gen/c/efuse_interface_ctrl.h:153`); no program read-back has been issued in this testcase, so the register must still hold it. That reset is `0x0` and the field is `sw = r`, so this compare is a transport/decode probe and **not** a liveness proof: a dead or unmapped window satisfies it equally. Since the field is driven only in the program FSM's `ST_CAPTURE_DATA` state ("Hardware debug only", `efuse_interface_ctrl.rdl:158`), which this testcase never enters, no distinguishing value can be placed in it. Block liveness is instead proven on the writable sibling `EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` @0x14 (`sw=rw`, `rdl:180`, non-zero reset `0x800000`) by a reset read plus a write/read-back/restore in the same run ([NEGATIVE-NEEDS-POSITIVE-CONTROL]).
* `SCRATCH_COLD_WARM_0` -- its own reset value 0, backed in the same run by a write/read-back positive control proving the register can hold a one ([NEGATIVE-NEEDS-POSITIVE-CONTROL]).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-BND-BASIC` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-BND-HOLD` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-BND-MAP` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-BND-REL` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-BND-STAY` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_boundary_signals_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-BND-BASIC`, `CHK-EFUSE-BND-HOLD`, `CHK-EFUSE-BND-MAP`, `CHK-EFUSE-BND-REL`, `CHK-EFUSE-BND-STAY` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-092-smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test]]
===== 92 `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI), ocah_jtag_vip

*Objective*: JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY unlocked write silent; locked write/read pulse `tb_efuse_locked_access_irq`.

*Test Coverage*:

* Locked eFuse shadow access raises `tb_efuse_locked_access_irq`.
* DECLARED PRECONDITION -- the read-lock is supplied by the bench, not the DUT.
* `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY` is read-locked before this sequence does anything because word 0 of `hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/smc_efuse_default.hex` has `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY_READ_LOCK` set (LOCKS bit 1), and the adopter-supplied simulation stand-in for the OTP macro, `hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/efuse_bank_model.sv`, `$readmemh`s that asset into the bank at time 0 under `+smc_efuse_hex` (named on this testcase's `[[tests]]` entry in `hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/holdout.toml`).
* The read-lock leg therefore proves that the DUT ENFORCES a lock it found already set; it does not prove a lock can be established through the fuse-programming path.
* The write-lock leg does establish its own lock, through the real `LOCKS` CSR write path.
* Everything asserted below -- lock enforcement, the non-disclosure property and the interrupt itself -- is produced by the design under test.
* The read-locked read is not value-compared: `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc` fixes `0xbadcab1e` only for the JTAG lifecycle-demux error slave and does not state what a read-locked shadow register returns over the CSR path, so that leg claims the interrupt pulse and non-disclosure (`lock[0] = 1` is read-locked) and nothing about the substituted word.
* The model contributes the fuse CONTENT only, and every expectation that depends on that content is derived from the asset at run time rather than hand-transcribed.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-RD` |The read of the read-locked `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY` pulses `tb_efuse_locked_access_irq` exactly once and the word it returns is neither the fuse content nor the pattern the unlocked write attempted (sequence asserts). The word itself is not value-compared: no document fixes what a read-locked shadow register returns over the CSR path (`smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-UNLOCK` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-WR` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-PRE` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-SCOREBOARD` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-RD`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-UNLOCK`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-IRQ-WR`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-PRE`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-SCOREBOARD` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-094-smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test]]
===== 94 `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Fuse sense + shadow read + PROGRAM command/status through DUT CSRs.

*Test Coverage*:

* Real fuse sense completes without `+skip_fuse_sense`, and the SMC_EFUSE_MAP window serves the sensed word over SEP_IN AXI (`efuse_shadow_regs.sv:674-679`, loaded by the sense FSM at `efuse_shadow_regs.sv:512-540`).
* The eFuse interface controller issues a PROGRAM command, latches the readback comparison result and reports it in `PROGRAM_STATUS` (`efuse_interface_shim.sv:433` -> `efuse_program_interface.sv:160`), with both polarities observed: set on the injected failure, clear on the clean program.
* `EFUSE_STATUS.efuse_sense_done` mirrors the sense handshake.
* The bank model's storage is unchanged by a fail-injected program and sticky-ORs one bit on a successful one. This shows the controller's command reached the bank; it says nothing about fuse physics.
* Foundry OTP macro analog timing / voltage.
* Full OCCP ROM secure-boot stack (see smc_occp_sanity_secure_error_test).

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]

|Checker |Description

|`CHK-EFUSE-OTP-PROGRAM-FAIL` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-OTP-PROGRAM-OK` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-OTP-SHADOW` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-EFUSE-OTP-STATUS` |Named compare in `smc_efuse_otp_burn_shadow_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-OTP-PRELOAD`, `CHK-EFUSE-OTP-PROGRAM-FAIL`, `CHK-EFUSE-OTP-PROGRAM-OK`, `CHK-EFUSE-OTP-SHADOW`, `CHK-EFUSE-OTP-STATUS` hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-103-smc_gpio_filter_access_sep_test]]
===== 103 `smc_gpio_filter_access_sep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_gpio_filter_access_sep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI), ocah_axi_vip (AXI-Lite)

*Objective*: GPIO0/1 ACCESS_FILTER: AxPROT=1 allowed, AxPROT=0 refused (read + write).

*Test Coverage*:

* READ half -- an unprivileged read is refused with DECERR, the code the GPIO programming guide specifies for a blocked transaction, and the error-slave signature 0xBADCAB1E, while the privileged read returns the programmed word.
* WRITE half -- an unprivileged write of the value that would DISARM the filter is refused, and a privileged read afterwards shows the register unchanged, so the refusal is proven to have taken no effect. The refusal code is asserted as "an error response" rather than an exact code: the GPIO programming guide (`hw/ip/gpio/doc/programming.adoc`, "Filter Configuration") specifies DECERR for a blocked transaction, which the read half requires exactly, while the write half is observed at the SEP_IN AXI port as SLVERR and no document this bench can cite fixes how the SMC fabric forwards a write-channel refusal.

*Test Procedure*:

[[sec-107-smc_gpio_multi_sample_test]]
===== 107 `smc_gpio_multi_sample_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_gpio_multi_sample_test`

*BFM / Model*: SEP_IN AXI driver (no external VIP)

*Objective*: Run the SMC OSS GPIO multi-sample observability scenario.

*Test Coverage*:

* runs `smc_probe_positive_control.prove_gpio_pad_bus_probe` first (through `ensure_gpio_pad_bus_control`), which programs GPIO wrap 0 as a TX output over SEP_IN AXI, requires exactly one new pad output-enable bit, requires that pad's value to track the register 1 -> 0, and requires a bit-for-bit restore of both vectors. Every leg is a bounded poll whose expiry is a failure;
* then takes the three spaced samples. Sample 0 is the reference; samples 1..N-1 carry its `core2pad_en_vec` as `expect_core2pad_en_vec`, and the scoreboard books that exact compare as *checked* evidence because step 1 credited the probe in this same run.

Drive `smc_gpio_multi_sample_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]

|Checker |Description
