`allow_timeout=True` stays a reviewed exception: helpers in
`smc_csr_seq_utils.py` that set it score the timeout, and each live
call site keeps a rationale comment (README gate). Force / placeholder
paths receive no credit.

Depth groups `reset` / `clock` / `irq` remain debug breadth. They are
not an extra feature-credit set.

On Verilator and Xcelium the `och_prim` `prim_sync2` / `prim_sync3` cells are
replaced by the behavioural stand-ins in `tb/verilator_stubs/`; VCS drops them.
Every leaf of
`reset.toml`, `clock.toml` and `irq.toml` has a row in the *Bench stand-ins*
section of <<smc-deferred-disposition>> stating which crossing sits on its
proof path, what its verdict reads, and the result of a VCS run on the product
cells. Two runs supply those results. The group control run (`--items reset
clock irq`) covered the 35 leaves of the `reset`, `clock` and `irq` groups: 34
entries of the three files plus `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test`, an
`irq` group member defined in `combined.toml`. The five entries the three
files hold outside those groups -- `smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test`,
`smc_flr_sanity_test`, `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test`,
`smc_zeroer_dma_timeout_test` and `smc_mailbox_irq_test` -- were not in it;
the separate run `20260914_031352__vcs__standin_files_minus_groups`, with the waiver
below in place, grades them 5 of 5 PASS on both the cocotb verdict and the
runner, with no assertion firing after time 0. Two findings of the group
control run belong to this signoff. The first
is closed: the VCS flow graded every leaf FAIL on a time-0 PeakRDL external-ack
assertion class (`assert_bad_ext_wr_ack` / `assert_bad_ext_rd_ack` in
`cpu_ctrl_reg`, `reset_unit_reg` and `system_timer_octs_reg`) that fires once
before reset, while the ack strobes are still X, and that the cocotb verdicts
do not depend on. That class is waived at time 0 only, in `smc_sim_cfg.toml`
`[pass_fail]` in the form SEP uses for `abr_reg`, and the waived re-run
`20260914_035111__vcs__reset` grades 35 of 35 PASS. The second stays open:
`smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test` fires the product assertion
`CheckMutualExclusionClk1` in `avsbus_controller.u_refclk_apbclk_mux`
(`prim_ag_clk_mux.sv`) at 4688 ns on seed 1611842931. The waiver does not
cover it -- a replay of the leaf on that seed grades FAIL on that line -- and
no Verilator run can observe it because assertions are compiled out there.
That firing remains an open item of this signoff; the condition that closes
it, and the tracker for it, are the *Closes when* cell of the `prim_sync2/3`
control row in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>.

The cool-reset OUT names (`smc_cool_reset_from_primary_chiplet_test`,
`smc_cool_reset_from_bmc_test`, `smc_cool_reset_x_cool_reset_test`) have rows
in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>.

=== Known Limitations

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact |Resolution Path
|The SEP_IN local fabric folds every request into the `LOCAL_BASE`-aligned, `REGION_SIZE`-byte alias aperture (`SMC_BASE_CONFIG.REGION_SIZE`, reset `0x0100_0000`, so `addr[23:0]` survive and `LOCAL_BASE` is prefixed above them), so the cluster-local window `0xC800_xxxx` answers from `0xC000_xxxx` |No access from this testbench reaches a Bus Error Unit; `smc_cluster_beu_test` locks the address fold instead |By design: the SEP and SMU fabric decodes prevent such an address reaching the port. BEU behaviour needs a path that bypasses the mask
|Verilator is a 2-state simulator |Resolvability predicates (`is_resolvable`) cannot fail on a Verilator run, so X-propagation claims are not closed there |Retain a VCS or Xcelium run for the scenarios whose evidence depends on 4-state behaviour
|PLL and PVT are placeholder RTL in this harness |The PLL/PVT scenarios in the wider plan cannot be run: there is no design behind the register interface |Needs the adopter's PLL/PVT blocks integrated
|The I3C DAT/DCT RAM is not present in `tb_top` |CCC/IBI scenarios that need device-address-table state cannot run; `smc_i3c_to_fabric_test` is decode-only |Add a DAT/DCT model, or drive the sequences from a full I3C host model
|The DTP CSR window has no responder |AXI-Lite accesses to it would wedge, so the boundary is left idle rather than answered by a placeholder |Connect the DTP CSR subordinate; SMU already connects the same port
|The cluster PLIC aperture (0xC400_0000) is not writable from SEP_IN AXI |No *cocotb* testcase can cover the PLIC register file from the external port: PRIORITY[1] accepts a write with OKAY and stores nothing, and the +2 MB context pages return non-OKAY. The measurement is recorded against `smc_cluster_plic_csr_test` in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>; the four register-level properties -- context independence across the eight threshold pages, source 0 and PENDING read-only, PENDING tracking a real interrupt -- need a write path. The PLIC's *function* is covered: `smc_fw_plic_claim_test` programs it from the CPU and claims through it |Expose a write path from SEP_IN, or extend the firmware image to read back what it programmed
|SV covergroups are not supported by Verilator |Functional coverage is Python-side on the open-source flow |See `SMC_FCOV.adoc`
|`smc_deadspace_decode_test` measures wrap-to-live aliasing: dead offsets in seven register windows wrap onto live registers with an OKAY response |Not in `all`; the card does not claim sign-off PASS. The leaf runs weekly in `rtl_issue` under `expect_fail`, so the defect has a graded measurement and the row goes red when the decode changes |Resolve the wrap-to-live aliasing decode contract (#585); the leaf then moves to its feature testlist with the `ci` tag
|`smc_state_fault_inject_test` reaches the illegal eFuse and Zeroer state encodings only by forcing the state flops through the `tb_*_state_inject` hooks in `tb_top` |The stimulus is a testbench force, not a product-port access. The leaf runs in `all` and `hosted` and scores the DUT's reaction over the CSRs, so its PASS is evidence for the fail-closed behaviour and not for the reachability of an illegal encoding from any port |Reach an illegal encoding from a product port, or accept the force as the only stimulus for this property, recorded here
|Thirteen test modules written against this bench are out of the tree |Their blockers are outside the test: adopter placeholder PLL/PVT RTL, a wrapper pin that does not exist, an idle DTP boundary, a PLIC aperture SEP_IN cannot write, a CLINT window SEP_IN cannot reach, TB glue, or a pad-level VIP the bench lacks. The retired-module table in <<smc-deferred-disposition>> names the blocker per row |Clear the blocker named in the row; the module is restored from git history
|The raw OTP window has no responder on `smc_wrapper` |No testcase exercises a permission check at the raw OTP window -- the OTP bank itself, as distinct from the eFuse-shim CSR window `smc_efuse_chip_config_read_test_seq` reads over SEP_IN and the JTAG access-control path `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test` drives. `smc_efuse_permission_boundary_test` stays on the CSR side of the eFuse boundary and claims only the chip-config reads it makes. The permission semantics this bench can reach are covered elsewhere: `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test` (lifecycle-gated JTAG-side access) and `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test` (locked-shadow access) |Add a raw-OTP responder to the bench, or assign the raw OTP permission path to the eFuse block-level TB
|`smc_octs_sanity_test` carries an OCTS name but drives the AVSBus status CSR path and the sideband observability probe |Its PASS is evidence for the AVS status window decode and the bench sideband probes, not for any OCTS register or pad; its protocol-VIP record is booked as a proxy. OCTS is covered by `smc_octs_dual_sync_test` (`dual`) and `smc_fw_octs_p0_primary_test` (`fw`) |Rename the leaf to what it drives, or give it OCTS stimulus and re-review the card
|`feat_ctrl` bit 37 `SMC_FUSE_TEST` is not consumed by SMC |The lifecycle-gated JTAG eFuse matrix cannot observe that bit; the connection stays a negative item |Cover the bit once SMC consumes it
|UART0 transmits two stop bits for a 5-bit word with `LCR.STB` set, where `uart_16550_main.rdl` describes 1.5 (#2121) |`smc_uart_baud_word_parity_format_test` measures the frame length on the TX pad for every format; for the two 5-bit `STB = 1` formats it admits either 1.5 or 2 stop bits, records which one was measured, and so does not claim the RDL count for them (one stop bit still fails). Every other format is held to the RDL count |Fix the transmitter or the RDL description under #2121, then drop the admitted alternative so the two formats are held to one count
|===

[[smc-held-out-testcases]]
==== Held-out testcases

Every leaf defined in `testlists/holdout.toml`. None is a member of `all`;
each card below states what a run of the leaf must show and records no pass.
The reproducers are the `rtl_issue` group:

Tests whose job is to fail. Each holds the runnable evidence for one RTL
defect, so the defect has a measurement rather than a description alone. All
are enrolled through `rtl_issue` in `testlists/holdout.toml` -- no `ci` tag, not
in `smoke`, `hosted` or `all`. Every `[[tests]]` entry carries `expect_fail` naming the issue and
`expect_fail_match` pinned to the assertion it raises; the weekly tier runs
the group on its own `regress.yml` row and grades the recorded failure as
PASS, so the row goes red (`expected_fail_passed`) the week a defect is
fixed and the leaf moves to its feature testlist. The member is
`smc_deadspace_decode_test` (#585); its card follows, with the other held-out
leaves.

Every one carries an arm leg that must pass before its defect leg is scored.
Without that, a stuck-at field or a dead probe would satisfy the defect leg for
the wrong reason.

[[sec-026-smc_deadspace_decode_test]]
===== 26 `smc_deadspace_decode_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_deadspace_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Probe wrap-period offsets past PeakRDL SIZE and watch live CSRs.

*Test Coverage*:

* Prove whether SMC register windows alias unmapped offsets onto live CSRs.
* Golden legality is PeakRDL `SIZE` / `TOTAL_SIZE` read by symbol from `smc_addr.h` (`_block_extent` + `DeadspaceProbe.__post_init__`), never the xbar window and never a hand-copied literal.
* A dead write that wraps onto a live register is the defect under test.
* SCOPE -- OKAY-into-void: a write into unmapped space that returns OKAY and leaves the live register alone is tallied as ACCEPTED and is OUT OF SCOPE for eight of the nine probes; only `i2c0_intr_enable` carries `expect_refuse` and asserts on it, because the i2c_wrap SIZE range-check is a decode contract this testcase can hold the DUT to.
* For the other eight no authority establishes that the SMC xbar must DECERR an unmapped offset rather than silently accept it, so `accepted=N` in the summary is a reported tally and not a verdict.
* No document in the tree fixes that contract for the whole map; this testcase does not decide it.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_deadspace_decode_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|— |This leaf emits no `CHK-*` token. Fail paths are the sequence asserts.
|===


*Pass Criteria*: not a sign-off PASS. The leaf measures wrap-to-live aliasing (dead offsets in seven register windows wrap onto live registers with an OKAY response) and runs weekly in `rtl_issue` under `expect_fail`, with `expect_fail_match` pinned to the `wrap_to_live` / `read_alias` assertion; the observed failure grades PASS and an observed PASS grades `expected_fail_passed`. Fail paths are the sequence asserts. Known Limitations records the hole. Enrolled in: `rtl_issue` only -- an `rtl_issue` reproducer graded as an expected failure on the weekly tier (see <<smc-held-out-testcases>>). This card records what a run must show, not a pass.
'''''





[[sec-161-smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test]]
===== 161 `smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test`

*BFM / Model*: no model on either side -- `u_bfm` runs the `occp_unsecure_boot_test` DV ROM image against `u_dut` running the production boot ROM

*Objective*: OCCP unsecure boot end to end between two real SMCs over the shared I3C bus.

*Test Coverage*:

* Payload staging into the controller's scratch SRAM over its inbound AXI manager.
* The scratch 5-8 host protocol between testbench and controller firmware.
* ENTDAA, chunked OCCP `WRITE`s and the `JUMP`, firmware to firmware.
* Real traffic on the shared I3C bus, counted by the testbench rather than reported by firmware.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Hold the controller parked on its target-ready handshake so the staging window stays open.
. Draw the staging address and the transfer destination per run from the OCCP window, so honouring the WRITE address field and always writing from the bottom of the window are distinguishable.
. Stage the payload over AXI, seed scratch 5-8, and release both CPUs.
. Wait for the target ROM to reach its OCCP command loop, then let the controller drive the transfer and the JUMP.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCCP-TARGET-READY` |Target ROM is in its OCCP command loop, by POST code and trace
|`CHK-OCCP-PRECONDITION` |Target scratch 0 does not already hold the pass value before the transfer
|`CHK-OCCP-FW-SEED` |Controller RNG seeded from `RANDOM_SEED` and read back
|`CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-STAGED` |The payload reads back from the controller's SRAM exactly as staged
|`CHK-OCCP-HOST-PROTOCOL` |Controller scratch 5-8 reads back exactly as seeded
|`CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED` |Target SRAM at the drawn destination holds the transferred image -- first and last word, read through the `smc_scratch_map_pkg` backdoor that `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` holds against AXI. Raises on a mismatch
|`CHK-OCCP-BUS-ACTIVITY` |At least one I3C channel saw both an SCL fall and a START condition during the transfer, counted by the testbench rather than reported by firmware. Requiring the START rules out a clocking artefact moving `scl_falls` without a framed transfer
|`CHK-OCCP-CONTROLLER-NO-FAIL` |Controller scratch 0 is not `TEST_FAIL`
|`CHK-OCCP-JUMP-EXECUTED` |Target retired PC lands inside the transferred image
|`CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-EXECUTED` |Target scratch 0 holds `TEST_PASS`, written by the payload
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all ten checkers hold after their compares -- `CHK-OCCP-TARGET-READY`, `CHK-OCCP-PRECONDITION`, `CHK-OCCP-FW-SEED`, `CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-STAGED`, `CHK-OCCP-HOST-PROTOCOL`, `CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED`, `CHK-OCCP-BUS-ACTIVITY`, `CHK-OCCP-CONTROLLER-NO-FAIL`, `CHK-OCCP-JUMP-EXECUTED`, `CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-EXECUTED`. The payload destination is whatever `pick_payload_addresses` draws for that seed; the landing check compares first and last word at that drawn address.

`CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED` depends on `smc_scratch_map_pkg` decoding the drawn destination the same way `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` holds against AXI. `pick_payload_addresses` draws from anywhere in the OCCP window, so the peek is almost never reading offset 0, and a decode right only for the first 256 bytes of the window would look in the wrong bank. The six-offset agreement in `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` is what makes the checker able to raise.

Held out of `all` on runtime (hours of wall clock per run). Enrolled in: `occp_dual` only -- held out of `all` on runtime and run on demand as `occp_dual` (see <<smc-held-out-testcases>>). This card records what a run must show, not a pass.

'''''



[[sec-162-smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test]]
===== 162 `smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); production boot ROM built with `I3C_CORE=swap`

*Objective*: Production boot ROM reaches its OCCP command processing loop.

*Test Coverage*:

* The real ROM from reset through strap and fuse read, security checks, interface map and OCCP init.
* No bus traffic and no controller: this is the single-instance precondition the OCCP boot tests build on.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run with `+rom_bin64=prod_rom.bin64` and `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`, holding `boot_stall` from t=0.
. Program `RESET_VECTOR` before any core fetches, then release.
. Read the ROM's POST code from scratch 1 and require boot phase `[31:28]` to reach `OCCP_PROC`.
. Cross-check against CPU ROM fetch activity, so the phase cannot be credited to anything but the ROM executing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PROD-ROM-OCCP-PROC` |POST code reaches the OCCP command loop, corroborated by ROM fetch activity
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the POST code reaches `OCCP_PROC` and ROM fetch activity corroborates it. Real fuse sense is used -- no `+skip_fuse_sense` -- so the ROM reads straps and fuses itself. Enrolled in: `occp_boot` only -- held out of `all` on runtime and run on demand as `occp_boot` (see <<smc-held-out-testcases>>). This card records what a run must show, not a pass.

'''''

`smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test` has an entry in `holdout.toml` and no card: it
fails the rate comparison it was written to make (`clk_rst.adoc` places the OCTS
system timer in the reference clock domain; `smc_peripherals.sv` clocks it from
`clk_smc_i`), and is re-measured on demand.

[[smc-deferred-disposition]]
==== SMC deferred and OUT disposition

This is the disposition of record for every test name the package catalogues
without a module under `hw/sys/smc/dv/cocotb/tests/`, and for every enrolled
leaf that no scheduled tier runs (<<smc-scheduled-holdouts>>). Nothing here is
enrolled PASS. Every `@pyuvm.test()` module under `cocotb/tests/` is in at
least one testlist (`smc_base_test` and `smc_dual_base_test` are base classes,
not tests); a catalogued name without a module is one of two kinds:

* A *retired test module* targets this bench and its blocker is
  outside the test: adopter placeholder RTL, a wrapper pin that does not
  exist, an idle boundary, an aperture or window SEP_IN cannot write or
  reach, TB glue, or a VIP the bench lacks. Its code, and
  the `seq_lib/<name>_seq.py` sequence where it had one, is in git history
  and is restored when the blocker named in its row clears.
* A *catalog-only name* never had a module here: a commercial alias whose
  intent a live enrolled name already proves, or a body that needs stimulus,
  RTL or a firmware image this bench does not have.

Buckets:

* `adopter-pending OUT` — waits on adopter IP, a product pin, firmware/ROM, or a TB terminator.
* `child-TB-owned` — protocol completeness belongs to a block-level TB (I3C, DTP).
* `waived` — fake BFM, TB-glue, or a commercial alias already covered by a live name.

[[smc-idle-boundaries]]
===== Idle smc_wrapper boundaries (OUT)

These ports have no TB terminator on `--dut smc` / `smc_wrapper`
(`hw/sys/smc/dv/models/README.md`). They are OUT rather than terminated by a
placeholder responder. Block-level I3C and DTP signoff stay on those TBs;
do not count a wrapper terminator as that proof.

[cols="2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Boundary |Disposition |Reintroduction trigger
|I3C DAT/DCT |adopter-pending OUT |Real DAT/DCT macros on `smc_wrapper` / `smu_wrapper`. Protocol CCC/IBI remains I3C block DV. Do not credit `smc_i3c_to_fabric_test` as this proof.
|DTP CSR AXI-Lite |adopter-pending OUT |A real DTP CSR slave on `smc_wrapper`, or accept SMU-level DTP tests as the only wrapper-adjacent surface. SMU already wires DTP internally.
|===

[[smc-bench-tie-offs]]
===== Bench tie-offs

A tie-off is a constant on an `smc_wrapper` input port in `tb_top.sv` (the
single-instance `u_dut` and the `smc_dual_inst` wrapper instance). Every
`--dut smc` / `smc_wrapper` and SMC_DUAL leaf runs under it, on every tool.
Each row states the behaviour the constant keeps out of reach, the enrolled
claims that would change if the pin were driven, and the control that closes
the row.

[cols="2,1,5,3,4,1,1",options="header"]
|===
|Tie-off |Value |Behaviour made unreachable |Tests whose claims would change |Control / closes when |Owner |Review date
|`sep_security_disable_i` (`tb_top.sv`, both `smc_wrapper` instances)
|`1'b0`
|The eFuse sense bypass (`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Security Disable Bypass Mechanism"). On the product the pin is the SEP `security_disable_o` token-match result (`sep.sv`, wired through `smu.sv`); inside SMC it reaches only `smc_efuse_wrapper` -> `efuse_interface_controller.security_disable_i`. SMC builds the controller with `HAS_LC_STATE=0` (`smc_efuse_wrapper.sv`), so the bypass takes the non-LC branch: the controller drives `shadow_regs_o` from the shadow registers whenever fuse sensing has finished or the pin is high, and drives all zeros otherwise (`efuse_interface_controller.sv:449`); its command mux routes the shadow-register sense FSM to the bank only while sensing is not done and the pin is low (`efuse_interface_controller.sv:759`). With the pin at 1 the FSM's reads are never routed, so `fuse_sense_done` never rises, the shadow registers keep their all-zero reset value and are exposed unsensed, and `fuse_reset_n` -- and with it `rst_warm` (`smc_reset_ctrl.sv:135`) -- never release: the warm domain never leaves reset. With the pin at 0 every run senses the bank, which is what the product does on a chip whose SEC_DIS token did not match.
|None of the enrolled claims: no leaf claims the bypass, and every eFuse leaf proves the sensed path, which is the path a 0 selects. Were the pin driven to 1 for a run, every sequence that calls `wait_fuse_sense_done` (40 sequence files under `cocotb/seq_lib/`; `smc_base_test_seq.py` defines the method and each leaf that needs the warm domain calls it itself) would time out on `tb_fuse_sense_done`, and every warm-domain CSR access would hang with no ready.
|Positive control: a leaf that drives the pin to 1 and requires `fuse_sense_done` to stay 0 while the shadow-register window still answers. It must stay in the cold domain: the eFuse controller sits under `rst_primary`, ahead of `rst_warm`, so its shadow reads through the eFuse CSR window -- over the OTP JTAG AXI-Lite port or the fabric path -- are the only thing that answers under bypass; nothing in the warm domain does. It needs a driven pin (a bench input in `smc_tb_signal_list.svh`, idle 0) in place of the constant. Closes when that leaf is enrolled, or when SEP-integrated signoff (SMU with SEP=1) is accepted as the only place the token-match path is exercised and that acceptance is recorded here.
|minshaoho (`9282d8c14`)
|2026-12-15
|===

[[smc-bench-standins]]
===== Bench stand-ins

A stand-in is DV-owned RTL that answers in place of a product cell or an
adopter block. Two sit on proof paths of this bench. Each has an inventory
row, a control row, and -- where the stand-in can supply a verdict -- one row
per enrolled leaf stating which crossing is on its proof path, what the
verdict reads, and the result of the control. `hw/sys/smc/dv/README.md`,
`tb/verilator_stubs/README.md` and `models/README.md` describe layout and
enrolment and point here; the dispositions live only here.

Kinds used in the leaf tables:

* *reads the crossing* -- the verdict samples the stand-in's output itself
  (a CSR field or a `tb_top` tap wired to `q_o`). On Verilator that value has
  the stand-in as its author until the pipe has filled.
* *downstream* -- the verdict reads product state that the DUT derives from
  the crossing's output.
* *upstream* -- the verdict reads the near side; the crossing carries the
  value away and nothing behind it is read.
* *none* -- no `prim_sync2` / `prim_sync3` is on the proof path. Listed
  because the audit's reset, clock and IRQ buckets signed off as a whole.

[cols="2,3,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Stand-in |Replaces |Enrolled by |Tools it reaches
|`tb/verilator_stubs/prim_sync2.sv`, `prim_sync3.sv`
|`hw/common/och_prim/rtl/prim_sync2.sv` / `prim_sync3.sv`: `prim_flop_2sync` / `prim_flop_3sync` behind `prim_sync_randomized_delay`. `RANDOM_DELAY_ENABLE` is not defined on any DV build (`SYNTHESIS` is defined only on `[targets.dual]`), so `prim_sync_randomized_delay` passes its input through and the product cells are also a plain flop pipe; the one behaviour the stand-in adds is `initial '0` on every stage, a defined `0` on the far side before the pipe has filled where the product cell shows X on a four-state simulator.
|`smc_sim_cfg.toml` `[build].stubs`, emitted ahead of the Bender filelist so Verilator's `-Wno-MODDUP` first-definition-wins picks them. The product cells stay in the same compile: the generated Bender filelist `build/smc_bender.f` (`[build].bender_filelist`).
|Verilator and Xcelium compile them; VCS does not. The runner (`tools/dv/runlib/stages.py`) filters `[build].stubs` only when `tool == "vcs"`: there it drops any stub whose basename the Bender graph supplies, so the VCS compile list carries no `prim_sync2/3` stub and the product cells elaborate. For every other tool in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` `tools` -- Verilator and Xcelium -- it keeps both files and emits them ahead of the Bender filelist, so their compile lists carry the stub and the product cell together. Verilator's `-Wno-MODDUP` first-definition-wins selects the stub; no Xcelium build of this bench is recorded, so which of the pair Xcelium elaborates is not established by this record.
|`models/axil_okay_slv.sv` (behind `models/pll_wrap.sv` and `pvt_wrap.sv`)
|Nothing in the product RTL. It terminates the adopter PLL and PVT AXI-Lite windows that a real integration fills with the adopter's CSRs, completing every access with `RESP_OKAY` and all-zero read data. pulp `axi_err_slv` cannot hold that place: it asserts at time zero on every simulator but Verilator.
|Bender `smc_wrapper` target: `hw/top/smc_ip_integration.sv` `u_pll_wrap` / `u_pvt_wrap`.
|Every tool.
|===

[cols="2,4,1,1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Stand-in |Control that keeps the claims honest |Owner |Review |Closes when
|`prim_sync2/3`
|Height-3 control: the `reset`, `clock` and `irq` groups run on VCS with the product cells, `python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut smc --items reset clock irq --tool vcs --regress`, VCS X-2025.06_Full64, 35 leaves; the VCS compile list carries the product `prim_sync2.sv` / `prim_sync3.sv` from `smc_bender.f` and no stub. Run `20260914_022740__vcs__reset`, before the waiver: cocotb verdict 35 of 35 PASS with every `CHK-*` token of each card present; runner grade 35 of 35 FAIL, all on one message class -- the PeakRDL immediate assertions `assert_bad_ext_wr_ack` / `assert_bad_ext_rd_ack` in `cpu_ctrl_reg`, `reset_unit_reg` and `system_timer_octs_reg` (six sites) each report once `at time 0 fs`, before any reset, while `arst_n` and the ack strobes are still X; no leaf reports them at a later time. That class is waived in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` `[pass_fail]` in the form SEP uses for `abr_reg`: an ignore pattern naming exactly these six assertions together with the `at time 0 fs` text, paired with a hard-fail pattern that turns a later firing of the same assertions into an error. Run `20260914_035111__vcs__reset`, with the waiver committed and the tree clean (`result.json` `git.dirty = false`): cocotb 35 of 35 PASS, runner 35 of 35 PASS, the six time-0 lines recorded as `IGNORED` evidence in every leaf and no `Error:` line at any later time. One product assertion fires at a non-zero time and is not waived: `prim_ag_clk_mux.sv` `CheckMutualExclusionClk1` in `avsbus_controller.u_refclk_apbclk_mux` at 4688 ns in `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test`, the DFT `test_en_i` bypass leaf, on seed 1611842931 (the control run's seed). The waived regression drew seed 1024727039 and did not reach it; a replay of the leaf on seed 1611842931 with the waiver in place (`20260914_035535__vcs__dft_seed1611842931`) fires it again and is graded FAIL on that line, so the firing is seed-dependent and the waiver does not cover it. Verilator compiles assertions out, so only a commercial run can see it. Neither observation depends on the synchroniser cells. The group selection excludes five entries of the three testlist files (named above the reset-group leaf table); run `20260914_031352__vcs__standin_files_minus_groups` covered those five on the same VCS build: cocotb 5 of 5 PASS, runner 5 of 5 PASS, no assertion after time 0.

====== prim_sync2 / prim_sync3 crossings in the DUT

`smc_peripherals` instantiates `smc_peripherals_cdc` with `SYNC_STAGES = 3`, so
its crossings are `prim_sync3`. Under `smc_wrapper` the stand-ins replace:

[cols="3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Instance |Carries |Read by the bench through
|`u_smc_base.u_ext_interrupts_sync3` |`ext_interrupts_i` into `clk_smc`, then `cpu_interrupts_o` |`tb_ext_interrupt_0_sync` and `tb_temp_interrupt_irq` (both wired to the synchroniser output); the cluster PLIC
|`u_smc_base.u_ext_debug_bus_sync3` |`ext_debug_bus_i` |nothing: `tb_top` ties the input to `'0`
|`smc_subsystem_resets.u_reset_complete_sync` |`ss_reset_complete_i` (32 bits) |`SS_RESET_COMPLETE` CSR
|`smc_cool_reset_wrap.u_isolate_req_pin_sync`, `u_rst_cool_ni_sync_smc`, `u_rst_cool_no_sync_smc` |pin and reset visibility |`ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` CSR. The reset tree itself takes `rst_cool_ni` through the `clk_ref_i` de-glitch in `smc_reset_ctrl`, not through these.
|`smc_cool_reset_wrap.u_cfg_flr_pf_active_sync_smc`, `u_cfg_flr_pf_active_sync_ref` |`cfg_flr_pf_active_i` into both domains; the FLR edge detect behind them drives `rst_cool_no`, `isolate_req_o` and skip-mem-repair |`tb_isolate_req_o`, `tb_rst_cool_from_flr`, reset observables
|`smc_peripherals.u_boot_stall_combined_sync` |pad-57 / JTAG boot stall into `clk_smc`, then `boot_stall_sticky` and the fuse-reset gate |`tb_boot_stall_combined_o` (`boot_stall_processed_o`); whether the CPU fetches
|`smc_peripherals.u_rst_ext_wdt_irq_sync` |`sep_wdt_reset_n_i` as an interrupt, `peripheral_interrupts[26]` |IRQ aggregates only; the reset effect of the same pin enters the reset unit directly
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_ndmreset_request_sync` |`ndmreset_request_i` into `clk_smc`; NDM handshake and `peripheral_interrupts[11]` |`tb_ndmreset_irq`, `tb_ndmreset_process`, `NDMRESET_*` CSRs
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_i2c_enable_sync`, `u_i2c_irq_sync`, `u_i2c_debug_sync`, `u_uart_enable_sync`, `u_uart_combined_irq_sync`, `u_i3c_irq_sync`, `u_avsbus_irq_sync` |peripheral enables and interrupts from the peripheral clock into `clk_smc` |`tb_i2c0_enable`, `tb_i2c_irq`, `tb_avsbus_irq`, `peripheral_interrupts`. `tb_uart_irq_any` reads `uart_interrupt_o`, the peripheral-clock side, not the crossing.
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_i2c_cg_en_sync`, `u_uart_cg_en_sync`, `u_avs_cg_en_periph_sync`, `u_i3c_cg_en_sync`, `u_avs_cg_en_ref_sync`, `u_tel_cg_en_sync` |clock-gate enables out of `clk_smc` |not read: every `tb_*_cg_en` tap is the `clk_smc` side
|`avsbus_controller.u_gate_refclk_en_sync`, `u_idle_clk_reg_resync`, `u_slave_resync_pending_resync`, `u_readback_fifo_full_resync`, `u_slave_unresponsive_resync`, `u_slave_interrupt_resync`, `u_avs_readback_en_resync`, `u_max_retries_attempted_resync`; the gray-pointer `prim_sync3` arrays of `cmd_async_fifo_inst` and `readasync_back_fifo_inst` (`avsbus_async_fifo`) |AVSBus internal resynchronisation and the command / read-back FIFO pointers |AVSBus state and CSRs
|`smc_cpu_ctrl_wrap` `prim_refclk_count_w_cdc` (two `prim_sync3`) |`REFERENCE_COUNTER` handshake across `clk_ref_i` / `clk_smc` |`REFERENCE_COUNTER` CSR
|===

No eFuse leaf crosses one: the eFuse reset synchroniser is the vendored
`prim_rst_sync`, compiled from source on every tool. The pulp `sync` shim
(`hw/common/sync.sv`, Bender `common_cell_sync_shim`) reaches `prim_sync2r` /
`prim_sync3r` for every caller in this build, so the `axi_cdc` FIFOs in
`smc_peripherals_cdc` are not behind a stand-in.

====== axil_okay_slv

The six retired consumers, each `adopter-pending OUT` in the table above, in
no testlist, and never reporting PASS: `smc_pll_pvt_clock_config_test`,
`smc_pll_dvfs_depth_test`, `smc_pll_cgm_awm_config_test`,
`smc_pvt_analog_sensor_test`, `smc_pll_awm_freq_sweep_test`,
`smc_pvt_droop_test`. Each reads the PLL or PVT window and expects
`OKAY` with data `0`, which is the value the stand-in authors, so the
expected and the actual value have the same author and the compare holds on
any RTL behind it, including none. The catalog-only PLL names
(`smc_pll_init_test`, `smc_pll_fuse_init_code_test`, `smc_pll_dvfs_test`,
`smc_combined_pvt_sanity_test`, `smc_pll_disabled_gpio_test`) and
`smc_macro_axil_routing_test` would sit behind the same terminator.

[cols="3,3,4,2",options="header"]
|===
|Consumer |What it proves |What it does not prove |Control
|`prove_axil_external_active_probe` (`cocotb/seq_lib/smc_probe_positive_control.py`), the same-run positive control declared by `smc_canonical_smoke_test`, `smc_axil_idle_test`, `smc_axil_burst_idle_test` and `smc_6agent_observability_test` (`probe_positive_controls`)
|Reachability: a frontdoor SEP_IN read of the PLL window drives `u_smc.smc_external_req_o.ar_valid` high, `tb_axil_external_active` samples 1 during it and returns to 0 after, so the idle `== 0` compares the scoreboard books on that probe are fail-capable. Both levels are DUT-driven.
|Any register value. The `rdata == 0` the control reads back is the stand-in's `RESP_DATA` constant, not a DUT reset value, and no card cites it. `smc_axi_monitor.py` keeps PLL and PVT out of its DECERR window list for the same reason: the windows answer OKAY by construction.
|The probe itself: `hits > 0` and the return to idle are asserted, and the run fails if the pulse is never seen. `smc_scoreboard.py` also asserts the read-back against `expected = 0`, the stand-in's constant; that compare holds on any RTL behind the window, including none.
|===

[[smc-scheduled-holdouts]]
===== Leaves no scheduled tier runs

Every SMC leaf either runs on a scheduled tier -- `smoke` per PR (`sim.yml`),
`hosted` nightly and weekly, `rtl_issue` weekly on its own row
(`regress.yml`) -- or has its hold-out recorded here with the reason and the
closing condition. The hosted runners carry Verilator, bender and uv only.
The `ocah-toolchain` image (`tools/docker/Dockerfile`: `gcc-riscv64-unknown-elf`
and `picolibc-riscv64-unknown-elf`) is built locally and published to no
registry, and no workflow builds firmware, so every firmware leaf below, each
of which runs a `c_compile` stage, is run on demand (`scripts/docker-run.sh`
builds the images) until CI has a toolchain. The `smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test`
row is held out for a different reason: it fails the comparison it was written
to make, and waits on a ruling.

[cols="2,1,3,1,1,3",options="header"]
|===
|Leaf |Groups |Why CI cannot run it |Owner |Review |Closes when

Its checkers read boot-sequencer and AXI signals only, so pointing `+rom_hex` at `assets/smc_rom_default.hex` and `+smc_efuse_hex` at `assets/smc_efuse_default.hex` and dropping `firmware` would make it toolchain-free; that is a test-behaviour change for the SMC owner, not this record

|`smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test` |`occp_dual` |`target = "dual"`, `firmware = dual_occp` (three images), `timeout_sec = 21600`: hours of wall clock while a full sram image crosses a ~2.6 MHz serial bus |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |Toolchain as above and a runner budget that fits it: its own weekly `items: occp_dual` row with a timeout near the six-hour hosted cap
|`smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test` |none |In `testlists/holdout.toml` (`held_out`), outside `all`, run on demand. It fails the rate comparison it was written to make: `clk_rst.adoc:37-39` places the OCTS system timer in the reference clock domain, `smc_peripherals.sv:945-947` clocks `system_timer_octs` from `clk_smc_i`, and on seed 608337836 TIMER_COUNT advanced 992 counts while 428 `clk_ref_i` and 1026 `clk_smc_i` edges elapsed over the same window, against a tolerance of 14. It keeps its `[[tests]]` entry in `cpu.toml` and runs on demand (`--items smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test`) |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |The clock-domain discrepancy is ruled: either `system_timer_octs` takes `clk_ref_i` and the leaf joins the `clock` group with the `ci` tag, or `clk_rst.adoc` is corrected and the leaf's expectation moves to `clk_smc_i`; if neither is ruled, the leaf joins `rtl_issue` under `expect_fail` with the rate assertion as `expect_fail_match`
|===

`dual.toml` is therefore in the toolchain bucket, not an "elaboration only"
bucket: all four of its leaves declare `firmware`. `dual` is the class group of the three leaves in `all`;
`--items occp_dual` selects the unsecure-boot leaf.

The `rtl_issue` reproducers are not hold-outs. Every entry carries
`expect_fail` and `expect_fail_match` (`hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/holdout.toml`),
the weekly tier runs the group on its own `regress.yml` row (artifact
`dv-run-smc-verilator-weekly-rtl_issue`, which the dashboard does not read),
and the runner grades the recorded failure as PASS; an observed PASS grades
`expected_fail_passed`, the signal to move the leaf into its feature testlist.
Member: `smc_deadspace_decode_test` (#585).
`smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test` is not a member: a read that hits
`EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` completes with `EFUSE_READ_CTRL.READ_STATUS` set, so
the leaf passes, proves `READ_STATUS` 0 -> 1 and runs from `efuse.toml` in
`all` and `hosted`.

[[smc-fw-disposition]]
==== SMC firmware image disposition

This is the disposition of record for every firmware image name under
`hw/sys/smc/dv/fw/tests/`. The shared engine (`hw/common/dv/fw/compile.mk`)
builds every `tests/<name>/` directory it finds, so being built is not a
consumer. A consumer is a testlist entry that stages the image: a
`firmware = "<name>"` key in `hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/*.toml` resolved through
a `[c_build.*]` block of `smc_sim_cfg.toml`, or an SMU DV `[c_build.*]` that
builds and stages it. Every directory in the tree has a row here, and the rule
for adding one is in `hw/sys/smc/dv/fw/README.md`: a new image is added
together with its consumer and its row.

Buckets:

* `enrolled` -- staged by a testlist entry in this tree. The row names the
  leaf and the `[c_build.*]` block.
* `external-consumer` -- staged only by another package: an SMU DV group
  outside SMU `all`, or a regression outside this repository. The image is
  kept for that consumer and is not counted toward this tree's coverage.
* `deferred` -- kept for a stated blocker. The row is the blocker and the
  condition that closes it; a closed row moves to `enrolled` with its leaf,
  or to `superseded`.
* `superseded` -- the property is proven by a live enrolled name, or the image
  duplicates another image that has its own row, or the image has no DUT
  property. There is no directory in the tree; the code is in git history under
  the image name, and the row is what a reader searching for that name finds.

The cocotb test names of this package have their own record,
<<smc-deferred-disposition>>; rows below that cite a waived cocotb alias
point at it. SMU's record of the tests that stage SMC images is
`hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`.

====== Shared sources with an external consumer

The build of the companion regression outside this repository resolves its
firmware includes against this tree's `fw/tests/` and `fw/include/`, so a
shared header has a consumer when that firmware includes it or when an
external-consumer image above includes it.

[cols="3,5",options="header"]
|===
|Source |Consumer
|`tests/cla_sanity_sequence.h`, `tests/uart_sanity_sequence.h`, `tests/reset_ctrl_sequence.h`
|Included by firmware of the companion regression outside this repository; `reset_ctrl_sequence.h` also by `reset_ctrl_sanity`. Closes with the last includer.
|`include/tt_smc_ecc.h`
|Included by `ecc_lint` and `ecc_pint`.
|`tests/coremark.h`, `tests/core_portme.h`, `tests/core_portme.c` and `FW_TEST_EXTRA_SRCS_coremark` in `fw.mk`
|The `coremark` image.
|`tests/cpu_perf.h`
|Included by `tests/core_portme.c` and the `coremark`, `matrix_mult`, `mergesort` and `quicksort` images. Closes with the last includer.
|===


===== Deferred images

Each row states the blocker and the consumer whose arrival closes it. The
`smc_fw_hello_world_test` pattern is a fire-and-forget CPU verdict on
`CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0`; the `smc_fw_plic_claim_test` pattern adds an `arm_value`
the cocotb side waits for before it drives a stimulus. Rows that need a
rom-mode OCCP build share one trigger: the generic `[c_build.*]` is added with
the first image that consumes it, not ahead of it.

[cols="3,2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Image |Bucket |Blocker and what closes the row
|i2c_p1_back_to_back_xfer
|deferred
|The header promises eight back-to-back transfers but `main()` performs one 4-byte write (Step 4) and posts PASS; a wire decoder on the pad bus saw one START and one STOP. Closes when the image runs the transactions its header describes and is enrolled as `smc_fw_i2c_p1_back_to_back_xfer_test` on the `smc_fw_i2c_pair_test_seq` wire-floor pattern.
|mailbox_sanity
|deferred
|PLIC claim of a mailbox IRQ is provable only from the CPU; `smc_mailbox_irq_test` and `smc_mailbox_event_irq_test` observe the IRQ pin. Closes with a `smc_fw_plic_claim_test`-pattern leaf (`arm_value` plus an `on_armed` stimulus from cocotb) in `testlists/fw.toml` whose stimulus writes the mailbox after the 'interrupts set up' word.
|wdt_sanity
|deferred
|Cluster WDT bark, stuck handler, second-stage reset and revive need a running hart; `smc_wdt_key_csr_test` (`testlists/power.toml`) is CSR-only. Closes with a `smc_fw_plic_claim_test`-pattern leaf (`arm_value` plus an `on_armed` stimulus from cocotb) in `testlists/fw.toml` arming on scratch[7].
|xtrigger_gpio_override
|deferred
|GPIO hw2 override of the 15 xtrigger channels, sampled on the pads after scratch[5] arms; the cocotb alias `smc_xtrigger_gpio_override_test` is catalog-only in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>. Closes with a `smc_fw_plic_claim_test`-pattern leaf (`arm_value` plus an `on_armed` stimulus from cocotb) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|rom_sanity
|deferred
|ROM window first/middle/last word decode; the cocotb alias `smc_dummy_rom_sanity_test` is catalog-only in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>. Closes with a `+rom_hex` asset carrying the three expected words and a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "rom_sanity"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=rom_sanity.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|rom_efuse_bits
|deferred
|ROM banks 1..3 decode and endian flip under eFuse scramble; the cocotb aliases `smc_rom_efuse_bits_0_test` / `_1_test` are catalog-only in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>. Closes with a scrambled four-bank `+rom_hex` asset, a matching `+smc_efuse_hex`, and a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "rom_efuse_bits"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=rom_efuse_bits.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|occp_comprehensive_error_verification_test
|deferred
|OCCP error codes and the SMC status ring against the status-coordination contract. Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_crc_err_injection_test
|deferred
|Header and body CRC fault injection, wire error plus status ring. Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_invalid_cmd_test
|deferred
|Invalid command code rejection and the status low byte. Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_ring_buffer_overflow_test
|deferred
|SMC status ring overflow (sole user of `include/smc_ring_buffer.h`). Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_zero_length_rw_test
|deferred
|Zero WLen/RLen -> `0x03 Invalid_header`. Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_zero_length_test
|deferred
|Zero-length operations and status records 0x120/0x130. Closes with a generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` that builds the production ROM plus `\{fw_target}`, added together with its first consumer, and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`).
|occp_boot_sequence_status_test
|deferred
|Boot status codes 0x001..0x021 in the SMC ring. OCCP master-BFM image. Closes after `occp_comprehensive_error_verification_test`, `occp_crc_err_injection_test`, `occp_invalid_cmd_test`, `occp_ring_buffer_overflow_test`, `occp_zero_length_rw_test` and `occp_zero_length_test` have their leaves, with the same generic rom-mode `[c_build.*]` and a `smc_occp_sanity_test`-pattern leaf on the dual bench (`testlists/dual.toml`); or moves to `superseded` when a live leaf proves the same property.

|ROM latches to the first interface; needs two controller interfaces into the target, and the dual bench shares one I3C0 bus. Closes when a second transport is wired into the dual bench.

|deferred
|Unlatch after the invalid-command threshold; same two-interface need. Closes when a second transport is wired into the dual bench.
|sep_ring_buffer_test

|SEP status ring overflow/underflow driven from cocotb. Needs that cocotb driver half. Closes with the driver on the dual bench, beside the OCCP BFM family.

|deferred

|sep_interop
|deferred
|SEP_SMU_002 two-sided mailbox handshake. SMC half of a two-sided SEP/SMU pair: the SEP half is `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smc_interop` and the pair is in no testlist, because SMC SRAM is CPU-private and a SEP-driven SMC bring-up cannot load it (SMU disposition bucket `SEP1-blocked`). Closes by SMU-owner agreement: enrolment of the pair in SMU DV, or removal of both halves.
|sep_mbox_irq
|deferred
|SEP_SMU_015 mailbox source IRQ consumer; SMC is the sole functional consumer of the eight SEP mailbox IRQs. SMC half of a two-sided SEP/SMU pair: the SEP half is `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smc_mbox_irq` and the pair is in no testlist, because SMC SRAM is CPU-private and a SEP-driven SMC bring-up cannot load it (SMU disposition bucket `SEP1-blocked`). Closes by SMU-owner agreement: enrolment of the pair in SMU DV, or removal of both halves.
|smu_smc_stall_sep
|deferred
|SEP_SMU_004 CLA halt/run of a live SEP. SMC half of a two-sided SEP/SMU pair: the SEP half is `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/smu_smc_stall_sep` and the pair is in no testlist, because SMC SRAM is CPU-private and a SEP-driven SMC bring-up cannot load it (SMU disposition bucket `SEP1-blocked`). Closes by SMU-owner agreement: enrolment of the pair in SMU DV, or removal of both halves.

|deferred
|SMC pad-arm for the xSPI GPIO override on the SMU external AXI path; SMU's `smu_sep_ext_axi_test` stages `smu_sep_ext_axi` instead. Closes by SMU-owner agreement: an SMU test that stages this image, or removal.
|i2c_p1_ackctrl
|deferred
|Programmable target ACK control; no cocotb body. Closes with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "i2c_p1_ackctrl"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=i2c_p1_ackctrl.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml` following the tier-1 `i2c_sanity` leaf.
|i2c_p1_adrmask
|deferred
|Dual target address plus address mask; no cocotb body. Closes with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "i2c_p1_adrmask"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=i2c_p1_adrmask.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml` following the tier-1 `i2c_sanity` leaf.
|i2c_p1_speedmode
|deferred
|Standard/Fast/Fast+ timing compliance; needs pad-timing observation from the TB. Closes with that observation and a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "i2c_p1_speedmode"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=i2c_p1_speedmode.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|i2c_p2_concurrent
|deferred
|Concurrent I2C instances; the shared SCL/SDA pad pair on this TB drives X under multi-master. Closes when each instance has its own pad pair.
|i2c_p2_mixmode
|deferred
|Controller and target in one instance; same shared-pad limitation. Closes when each instance has its own pad pair.
|cold_reset_lock_sanity
|deferred
|`RESET_UNIT.SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK`; the VPLAN card for `smc_reset_unit_lock_test` (`testlists/reset.toml`) cites this image as the firmware-side reference and the cocotb leaf proves the same two properties. Closes when the card cites the cocotb leaf alone, or with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "cold_reset_lock_sanity"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=cold_reset_lock_sanity.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|cool_reset_isolate_cfg
|deferred
|`ISOLATE_REQ_*` configuration; runtime scored by `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test`, and firmware cannot observe its own cool reset. Cited by `smc_reset_unit_lock_test_seq`. Closes when the sequence cites the cocotb leaf alone, or with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "cool_reset_isolate_cfg"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=cool_reset_isolate_cfg.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|avsbus_sanity
|deferred
|AVSBus controller command send; needs a pad-level AVSBus responder the bench lacks (the blocker of the waived `smc_sideband_avsbus_octs_bfm_test`). Closes with that VIP and a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "avsbus_sanity"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=avsbus_sanity.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|i2c_smbus_model_test
|deferred
|SMBus alert/suspend against an SV SMBus peer model this bench does not instantiate; `smc_smbus_alert_ara_test` and `smc_smbus_hostnotify_test` cover the VIP side. Closes with that model and a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "i2c_smbus_model_test"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=i2c_smbus_model_test.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`.
|mailbox_int
|deferred
|Four-hart mailbox IRQ handlers, the per-hart variant of `mailbox_sanity`. Closes after the `mailbox_sanity` leaf lands, with a decision whether the per-hart split adds a checker.
|uart_log_engine_multi_entry_rr
|deferred
|Round-robin across triggered entries. Closes with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "uart_log_engine_multi_entry_rr"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=uart_log_engine_multi_entry_rr.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml` after the tier-1 `uart_log_engine_single_entry` leaf proves the pattern.
|uart_log_engine_region_size
|deferred
|Per-entry slot address math. Closes with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf (`firmware = "uart_log_engine_region_size"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=uart_log_engine_region_size.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml` after the tier-1 `uart_log_engine_single_entry` leaf proves the pattern.
|uart_log_engine_intr
|deferred
|Log-engine `INTR_ENABLE` / `INTR_TEST` / `INTR_STATUS` truth table and W1C clear from the CPU. Its cocotb counterpart `smc_log_engine_intr_mask_test` (`testlists/uart.toml`, enrolled in `all`) proves from SEP_IN that `INTR_ENABLE` masks the output while `INTR_STATUS` latches and clears only on W1C, and passes; this image's expected table was written against the set-path gating RTL issue #1635 describes and is re-derived against that leaf's expectation before it is enrolled. Closes with a `smc_fw_hello_world_test`-pattern leaf `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_intr_test` (`firmware = "uart_log_engine_intr"`, `+smc_scratch_ram_hex=uart_log_engine_intr.ecc.hex`, `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`) in `testlists/fw.toml`, or by retiring this image in favour of the cocotb leaf that already proves the property.
